# Code Review Checklist

> ## ⛔ Disclosure rule — HARD REQUIREMENT
>
> **This is a public repository. Committed == published.** Every committed `.md` file — this checklist, `code-review-patterns.md`, and any doc under `.security/`, `.claude/`, or elsewhere — MUST NOT contain:
> - working exploit payloads or proof-of-concept strings,
> - step-by-step reproduction instructions, or
> - the full technical detail of any `open` (unfixed) finding.
>
> Committed files may contain ONLY: a one-line summary, severity, status, and file path — plus, for `fixed` issues, the general vulnerability class. **All exploit detail lives solely in the git-ignored reports.** When in doubt, write less in the committed file and keep the detail in the local report.
>
> ### "Fixed" means fixed in what users RUN, not on this branch
>
> `master` is 2.0 (unreleased); the line in actual use is `1.x`. A defect ported from
> 1.x is therefore **still live for every real install** after `master` is patched, so
> the "general vulnerability class" allowance above does **not** unlock it. Before
> writing a mechanism, a trigger, an affected feature, or an actor into any committed
> file, check whether `1.x` carries the same code (`git grep <symbol> 1.x`) — if it
> does, treat the finding as `open` for disclosure purposes no matter what `master`
> says. Caught on #74, where a 1.x-ported helper was described in detail in a
> `* Fixed:` bullet while `1.x` still shipped it.
>
> **This binds `CHANGELOG.md` and `readme.txt` hardest**, not least. They are the
> most-read files the project publishes — syndicated to wordpress.org and scanned by
> people looking for freshly-disclosed targets — so a changelog bullet reaches a far
> wider audience than a commit diff ever does. For a security fix that is not yet
> fixed everywhere: name the impact and the area, say detail is withheld pending the
> other line's fix, and stop there. Sequence the release so the 1.x fix ships first or
> together; the public diff discloses the mechanism the moment it is pushed.

Persistent coverage tracker for systematic reviews of the GTM4WP WordPress plugin. Updated after each review run.

**Companion files:** `.security/code-review-patterns.md` (what to look for) and `.security/threat-model.md` (**how to rate what you find** — the A0–A4 actor ladder and the lowest-actor severity rule). Read all three before a review.

**How to use:** Before running a review, read this file. **Reconcile the tree on disk against the tables below first** (pre-review step 1), then prioritize `[ ]` (unreviewed) cells. After the review, mark reviewed cells `[x]` with the date, record the reviewed sha, and append new findings to the Known Findings Log.

> ⚠️ **A component with no row is invisible, not unreviewed.** The Coverage Matrix can only signal a gap for a component it already lists — a new module is not `[ ]`, it is *absent*, and prioritization walks straight past it. This is not hypothetical: the **VisitorData** module and its **public** REST route landed 2026-07-16 with no row and were backfilled by hand on 2026-07-17. Always run the inventory step (`ls -d src/*/ src/Modules/*/`, plus the surface greps) and add missing rows **before** prioritizing. The same applies to the Public Surface Inventory below.

**Status markers:**
- `[ ]` — not yet reviewed
- `[x] YYYY-MM-DD` — reviewed on date
- `[~] YYYY-MM-DD` — reviewed but stale (files changed since)
- `[-]` — not applicable to this component

**Staleness rule:** A cell becomes `[~]` if any file in the component group was modified after the review. Check with `git log <sha>..HEAD -- <path>`, using the **`Reviewed at:` sha** recorded on the last report in the Known Findings Log. Use the sha, not `--since="YYYY-MM-DD"`: a date is imprecise in both directions when commits and the review land on the same day (Review 6 ran at 20:46 on a day with earlier commits, so `--since` reports those as changes-since and cannot distinguish a commit at 09:00 from one at 23:00).

**Dimensions:** *Cap/Nonce* = capability + nonce/CSRF on state changes · *Input San.* = `wp_unslash` + sanitize on request input · *Output XSS* = escaping into HTML/`<script>` (the primary dimension for this plugin) · *SQL* = `$wpdb->prepare` · *Cplx* = complexity/dead code · *Perf* = performance · *Types* = type hints/return types.

---

## Public Surface Inventory

Every externally-reachable entry point, the **lowest actor** who can reach it (threat model A0–A4), and the gate that stops the actor below. Maintained by the **New surface** sweep and pre-review step 1: a route not listed here has never been reconciled, which is the one gap the per-component matrix cannot express.

| Surface | Route / hook | Reachable by | Gate | Reviewed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Settings read/write | `gtm4wp/v2/settings` GET+POST (`RestController.php:69`) | A4 admin | `can_manage` (filterable cap) + `wp_rest` nonce via `api-fetch` | [x] 2026-07-28 |
| Settings export | `gtm4wp/v2/settings/export` GET (`RestController.php:100`) | A4 admin | `can_manage` + `wp_rest` nonce | [x] 2026-07-28 |
| Settings import | `gtm4wp/v2/settings/import` POST (`RestController.php:112`) | A4 admin | `can_manage` + `wp_rest` nonce; 512 KB / depth-16 caps, envelope marker, schema re-sanitization | [x] 2026-07-28 |
| Notice dismiss | `wp_ajax_gtm4wp_dismiss_notice` (`Notices.php:53`) | A4 admin (authenticated) | nonce + `current_user_can` (self-protecting since #18) + notice-id allow-list | [x] 2026-07-28 |
| Confirm purchase tracked | `gtm4wp/v2/confirm-purchase-tracked` POST (`PageDataLayer.php`) | A0/A1 guest or customer | **Origin/Referer match — the gate (#78)**, Referer read from `$_SERVER` only (#91) — plus a `wp_rest` nonce that is a malformed-request **filter, not** authorization. **Blessed FP-5**: order id from the WC session (`ProductData::PENDING_PURCHASE_SESSION_KEY`), request body ignored, marker consumed here (R7/#34 moved consumption off the GET). CORS: reflected grant stripped (`RestCors`, namespace-wide, unconditional since #97) | [x] 2026-08-04 (R12) |
| **Visitor data** | `gtm4wp/v2/visitor-data` GET (`VisitorDataEndpoint.php`) | **A0 unauthenticated** | `__return_true` — public by design; identity gates **verified in code** (R7, re-verified R9 + R10): user resolvers gate on `is_user_logged_in()`, Tier-2 reads own request headers, one-shot order id from the WC session only. Genuinely read-only (was #34); anonymous read sends no nonce (was #35). **Cross-origin reads withdrawn (#90)**: core's reflected CORS grant is stripped for this namespace, so a third-party page can no longer read the response with the visitor's cookies. | [x] 2026-08-04 |
| **Confirm re-add tracked** | `gtm4wp/v2/confirm-readd-tracked` POST (`PageDataLayer.php`) | A0/A1 guest or customer | **Origin/Referer match — the gate (#78)**, Referer read from `$_SERVER` only (#91) — plus the `wp_rest` nonce as a filter. **Blessed FP-5**: consumes only the caller's own session re-add marker, request body ignored (sibling of confirm-purchase, added R7 for #34). CORS: stripped by `RestCors` | [x] 2026-08-04 (R12) |
| **Namespace CORS policy** | `rest_pre_serve_request` prio 11 (`src/RestCors.php`) — not a route; the gate on every row above | — | Strips core's reflected `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`/`-Credentials`/`-Methods` for `gtm4wp/v2` on a foreign origin. **Registered unconditionally from `Plugin::boot()` since #97** — it was owned by `VisitorDataEndpoint::register_routes()`, which only runs when the cache-safe option is on, while the settings routes below put the namespace on every install. Since #102 it also covers the **namespace index** route (`/gtm4wp/v2`), which WordPress auto-registers and a `namespace/`-prefix test silently excluded | [x] 2026-08-05 (R13) |

> **A namespace has routes you did not register (2026-08-05, #102).** Every row in this
> table is a route the plugin declares, which makes it easy to believe the list is the
> namespace. It is not: WordPress auto-registers an **index** route for each namespace
> (`WP_REST_Server::register_route()` adds `'/' . $namespace`), so `gtm4wp/v2` answers a
> request this table never mentions. A namespace-wide control has to match the namespace
> **and** its own index, not just the `namespace/` prefix — and a prefix test reads as
> covering everything right up until you ask what the namespace root returns.

> **CORS is part of a route's gate, and nothing in this table used to say so (2026-08-04, #90).** WordPress reflects the request `Origin` with `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true` on **every** REST response by default, so "who can reach it" has a second half: who can *read the answer*. For a public route that is every website the visitor browses. When adding a row here, record the CORS posture alongside the permission callback — and remember that any token a route hands out is, by default, harvestable cross-origin.

It is not a route, and that is exactly why it had none: the row shape here is "route → gate", so a control that *is* a gate for several rows had nowhere to live and its registration condition was never written down. It turned out to be registered from one module's route callback, gated on that module's feature flag, while the settings routes keep the namespace alive on every install (#97, fixed). **When a gate is shared by several rows, give it its own row** — otherwise "who can reach it" is recorded per route while the thing that answers it is recorded nowhere.

> **Coverage note (Review 26, 2026-08-29):** reviewed `612122e..a33a9cc` — **6 commits, 19
> files, 5 production** — the post-meta allow-list + serialized skip, the `#190` JS parse
> guards, and the **2.0.0-rc1** bump. The first run in eleven whose range is *not* the previous
> review's fix session, so `ebc8ac1` was read as new production code rather than inherited.
> **1 Medium + 19 Low (#191–#210).** The Medium (#191) is the range's own tightening
> overshooting: a publish gate was moved onto a core predicate that returns a site filter's
> value verbatim, so a callback written for admin-UI visibility could publish an
> underscore-prefixed custom field to an **A0**-readable dataLayer — probe-confirmed, fixed by
> restoring the local rule as a floor and OR-ing the helper in (**RI-26**). Likelihood is
> bounded and was measured: **zero** `is_protected_meta` filter registrations across 121,728
> local PHP files — which bounds the original change's benefit exactly as much as its risk.
> **The adjudication stage did the run's real work:** 11 drafts verified, **6 recommendations
> refuted**, 2 dispositions refuted, 1 mechanism part-refuted, 1 draft dropped outright. Two of
> the refuted recommendations were the reviewer's own and would have shipped defects — one
> would have re-opened the very leak the range closes, one would have admitted `[]`, the
> likeliest bad value in its class. **Two recorded decisions failed re-derivation:** RI-24's
> audience premise (no denominator — here the ingredients ship in `1.x:CHANGELOG.md:141` since
> 2022-12-09, making the redaction ceiling zero, so #202 is a measured `wontfix`) and PA-19's
> prescribed pin (`gitCommitSha` unmoved across three plugin updates and 32 changed files).
> Both corrected in the patterns file; the playbook's plugin-scope glob was corrected too, and
> #193 is left **open** by maintainer decision because its clean fix changes a documented 1.x
> public JS API contract. Three directories that had never had a matrix row were added
> (`CLAUDE-SECURITY-*/`, `docs/`, `.roadmap/`), and the repo now carries its own `.gitignore`
> backstop for third-party scan output rather than relying on one that tooling writes.
> All nine sweeps re-derived **after** the fixes were staged; every figure reproduces
> (152 constants / 0 unread · 64 callbacks / 0 unresolved · 8 globals · 20+3+11 JS · 0
> wrappers · 12 `print_script_block` callers). Baseline: **PHP 2019 tests / 4742 assertions**
> (ordered + two random seeds), **JS 726 / 35 suites**, `phpcs` exit 0.
>

> **Coverage note (Review 23, 2026-08-12):** reviewed `5a1cc42..1165053` — **1 commit, 15 files, +406/−34** — which **is** R22's fix session in full, read deliberately per the standing note; the Medium and three of the five Lows came out of it, the **eighth** consecutive run that rule has paid. **1 Medium + 5 Low (#182–#187), no security finding** — nothing in range is reachable by any A0–A4 actor and the one D-axis item adds no reach. **The Medium is the story, and the adjudication stage *produced* it rather than filtering it:** a verifier dispatched to check a *documentation* claim about the new fallback's version window returned an unrelated **production defect in the same three lines** — an upstream filter re-applied with one argument where the vendor passes three. `WP_Hook` never pads a short list, so a site callback written to the documented signature raises an uncaught `ArgumentCountError` on the order-received page: fatal, not degraded. Generalized as **RI-25**, whose ledger is the point — **11** third-party hook sites, **10** correct, **1** wrong, and the wrong one was added by the very commit under review. **The suite structurally could not see it** (Brain Monkey intercepts `apply_filters`, so an optional-param stand-in passes either way, UC-3); the regression test declares its parameters **required** and was watched red with its trace on the finding's own line. **All 3 drafted recommendations were refuted**, two for shipping something worse than nothing — and #186 is the second consecutive run where a *verifier*, not the sweep, found the toolchain gap: the row counts **4** agent definitions where **28** resolve, 17 of the 24 uncounted declaring a write tool that none of the 4 counted do, because the playbook's inventory path is **relative** and cannot reach user scope. Because the diff was one commit the run went deep: all 8 sweeps re-run, every ledger reproducing; **RI-6 and RI-19's brand-new litmus run repo-wide for the first time** (45 feature guards, 0 findings); RI-21 re-verified at all 5 script-context sinks; the Perf cells (oldest at 2026-07-10) re-audited; **FP-4 re-derived** (rotation FP-1 R22 → **FP-4 R23**). Post-fix baseline: **PHP 1957 tests / 4581 assertions green** across three randomised-order runs, `phpcs` exit 0 repo-wide, `js/` and `build/` untouched (RI-9 N/A). **#183–#187 remain open.**
>
> **Coverage note (Review 22, 2026-08-12):** reviewed `bdf4b70..5a1cc42` — 8 commits, 15 files, +380/−63 — which **is** R21's fix session plus a contributor commit that landed *during* the run, read deliberately per the standing note; the Medium came out of it, the seventh consecutive run that rule has paid. **HEAD moved mid-review** (`b82d399` → `5a1cc42`) and was re-derived before the sha was written, which is R19's note being applied rather than rediscovered. **1 Medium + 8 Low (#173–#181).** The Medium is the only finding reachable by a site actor and it is **live, not a drift hypothesis**: a feature-availability guard on a delegated WooCommerce helper read "symbol unavailable" as "nothing to withhold", on a premise about versions that was taken from the *gate's* `@since` rather than the *helper's* — and the two differ, leaving a supported version window in which upstream hides the order while this plugin published the buyer's identity to an **A0** visitor holding a forwarded order-received URL. Its fail-open branch was executed **zero** times by the whole suite, because the stand-in class is defined unconditionally (UC-3, live). Generalized as **RI-19**'s second confirmation, with a new sub-shape: compare the `@since` of the *behaviour* with the `@since` of the *symbol* you delegate to. **The rest of the tier is this system reviewing itself, and three of them are defects in the range's own fix:** the verifier-isolation commit added scratch worktrees that were not git-ignored, so the isolation mechanism tripped the tree-clean check introduced beside it (#176), and a worktree without its own `vendor/` makes the suite silently exercise the **main** checkout while reporting green — hit independently by two verifiers in this run (#177). **The adjudication stage refuted 2 of 5 drafted mechanisms and 3 of the recommendations**, and the two refuted mechanisms were the same error in different clothes: reading a **correct** record as an error, once in a code comment and once in this file's own coverage notes. All 8 sweeps re-run; every ledger re-derived **after** the fixes were staged and all reproduce — 64 callbacks, 151 constants, 17 `wp_json_encode` call sites, 12 script sinks, 8 `global $…`, 20 bundles + 3 libs, 0 forwarding wrappers, `esc_js(` 28/13/15. **FP-1 re-derived** (rotation FP-5 R20 → FP-2 R21 → **FP-1 R22**); conclusion and its caller-side precondition both hold at all 12 callers. Post-fix baseline: **PHP 1956 tests / 4577 assertions green**, `phpcs` exit 0 repo-wide, `js/` and `build/` untouched (RI-9 N/A).
>
> **Coverage note (Review 21, 2026-08-12):** reviewed `d95f06a..bdf4b70` — 6 commits, 26 files, +2634/−904 — which **is** R20's fix session plus the order-received customer-data change at HEAD, read deliberately per the standing note; three Lows came out of it, the sixth consecutive run that rule has paid. **1 High + 1 Medium + 10 Low (#161–#172).** The High is **not a code defect**: it is a **disclosure** finding (#161) — committed release notes and an upstream registry row describe behaviour the released `1.x` line still carries, and the commit is pushed. That is #74's class in the files the disclosure rule says it binds hardest, and it is why this review's own rows for #161/#163 are deliberately terse. The Medium (#162) is on the **D-axis** and is the review's structural lesson: a pre-approved permission dispatching into a worktree-resolved tool config, live, in **user scope** — a permission file that applies to this project, sits outside the repository, appears in no diff, and had never been read because this sweep's own inventory names only the two project-scope files. **It was surfaced by a `finding-verifier`, not by the sweep.** The reviewed code itself is sound: `woocommerce_hides_order_from_visitor()` was checked against WooCommerce **trunk source** rather than its docblock and mirrors both gates exactly, and a hypothesised over-withholding divergence was measured and does not exist. All 8 sweeps re-run; every ledger reproduced with its stated counting rule — 64 callbacks, 151 constants, 20 bundles + 3 libs, **17** `wp_json_encode` call sites (5 script-context), 12 script sinks, 8 `global $…`, 0 forwarding wrappers. **FP-2 re-derived** (rotation FP-4 R19 → FP-5 R20 → **FP-2 R21**); conclusion and both stated properties hold. **Adjudication earned its keep: 5 of 6 drafted recommendations were refuted**, two of them by a verifier implementing the recommendation and measuring what broke — and one drafted *mechanism* was over-reached (#167 would have deleted a true, load-bearing UC-5 claim). One verifier collateral claim was refuted by the reviewer in turn. Baseline: **PHP 1952 tests / 4553 assertions green**, `phpcs` exit 0 repo-wide, `js/` and `build/` untouched (RI-9 N/A). Repository integrity after the parallel verifier run was re-verified and recorded in the report — see **#170**, which is why that is now a step rather than a courtesy.

> **Coverage note (Review 20, 2026-08-11):** reviewed `6fe789c..d95f06a` — 5 commits, 24 files, +1850/−212 — which **is** R19's fix session plus one `.gitignore` commit, read deliberately per R19's own standing note. Both Mediums came out of it, which is the fifth consecutive run where reading the previous review's fixes paid. **No Critical, no High, and no finding reachable by any A0–A4 actor**; the one security finding is on the D-axis. **2 Medium + 6 Low (#153–#160).** The first Medium is the review's whole story and it is a **regression inside #145's own fix**: replacing the five-entry trunk table with a generated 245-country table gave the 101 territories with **no** trunk prefix their own branch, and that branch returns *before* the "international form with the `+` left off" test the other 144 still get — so a Spanish, Portuguese, Greek, Danish or Czech customer typing their calling code without a `+` now has it prepended twice, length-valid and silent, where the pre-fix code got it right (#153, probe-verified across 5 territories). Neither test layer can see it: the 489-case generated oracle only ever builds the *national* spelling, and the hand-written case named for this exact behaviour asserts DE alone — the half where the property holds. The second Medium is **#154**, the project's first code **generator**, generalized as **PA-18**. **The Low tier is bookkeeping and documentation:** R19's `wp_json_encode` ledger and suite baseline were both invalidated by R19's own final fix commit (#155 — #116's shape one review after R15 wrote the rule against it), the generated table's docblock describes a 101-row branch as a 7-country special case (#156, and it is why #153 read as harmless), and the WooCommerce row label had not gained the new file (#157). All 8 sweeps re-run; ledgers re-derived with their counting rules — 64 callbacks, 151 constants, 12 script sinks, 11 expression-position `json_literal` sinks, 8 `global $…`, 20 bundles + 3 libs — every one reproduced **except** the one in #155. **FP-5 re-derived** (this run's turn) and **FP-1** as well, its blessed code being in the diff; both hold, all three FP-5 conditions now demonstrable in code. Baseline: **PHP 1461 tests / 4026 assertions green**, `phpcs` clean repo-wide, `js/` and `build/` untouched (RI-9 N/A).
>
> **Coverage note (Review 19, 2026-08-11):** reviewed `ffd2a63..6fe789c` — 5 commits, 41 files — **plus `ffd2a63` itself**, R18's fix commit, which its own entry handed forward to be read deliberately. **No Critical, no High, and no security finding at all**: nothing in range is reachable by any actor on the A0–A4 ladder, and the one D-axis item adds no reach. **1 Medium + 7 Low (#145–#152).** The Medium is the review's whole story and it is a *correctness* finding in brand-new code: the E.164 phone normalizer treats "this country is not in `NATIONAL_TRUNK_PREFIXES`" as "a leading `0` is a trunk prefix and can be stripped", but Italy has **no** trunk prefix and its leading `0` is part of the national significant number — so every Italian landline hashes to a value Google can never match, silently, which is the exact failure the commit was written to fix (#145, probe-verified, confirmed against Google's own libphonenumber FAQ, and it contradicts the claim in upstream row U110). **The Low tier is this system's oldest theme, three times over:** the commit that wrote "omit rather than send empty" into a comment left the identifier three lines above it sending empty (#146), the ledger that promoted the JSON guard to ten call sites left an eleventh (#147), and the base commit's method move left two docblocks naming a symbol it deleted (#149). Newly covered: the **`.gtm-container-template/`** directory, which had no row (#150) and needed one for two questions nothing else here asks — it is clean on both. Also new: **#148**, `phpunit-network.xml` is a tool config that reaches an executable via an explicitly-passed `-c`, which `tools/check-branch.js` does not watch — #101's class in the script written to prompt about #101, and #109's failure mode (false reassurance) a second time. All 8 sweeps re-run; ledgers re-derived with their counting rules and every one reproduced (64 callbacks, 151 constants, 20 bundles + 3 libs, 12 script-sink callers). Baseline: **PHP 962 tests / 3508 assertions green**, `phpcs` clean repo-wide.

## Whole-Repo Sweeps

New surface, dead code, and cross-file duplication are **whole-repo** concerns — they do not map onto the per-component Coverage Matrix. Log each sweep here with the date last run and a one-line result. Run via the playbooks in `.claude/commands/code-review.md` — § A for **New surface**, § B for the rest (grep-for-references, not eyeball). Treat a sweep older than ~4 weeks, or predating a significant feature landing, as stale.

| Sweep | Last run | Result summary |
|---|---|---|
| **New surface / public endpoints** (`register_rest_route`, `permission_callback`, `__return_true`, `wp_ajax_`, `admin_post_` reconciled against the Public Surface Inventory) | 2026-09-02 (R28) | **R28:** grep re-run at `7eba61f`: reconciles exactly with R23–R27 (6 `register_rest_route` / 7 `permission_callback` / 1 `__return_true` / 1 `wp_ajax_` / 0 `admin_post_`) across a 31-commit range that added a whole store-integration module — the EDD module declares **no** route, AJAX handler or gate. Inventory: **19** `src/` directories (+`Ecommerce/`, +`Modules/EasyDigitalDownloads/`, both given matrix rows this run), **21** frontend + 3 admin JS files. The shape this sweep had no term for this time: a **page-gate ported from another plugin's source** — the EDD success-page order resolution mirrors EDD core's receipt chain, and the ported copy is where R28's one High lives (#217): the resolver came over, the verification it feeds in upstream did not. The route-shaped grep cannot see a conditional-tag-gated dataLayer emitter whose authorization is prose ported from a third party; PA-10's entry now carries the lesson. — **R27:** grep re-run at `7f226b4`: reconciles exactly with R23–R26 (6 `register_rest_route` calls / 7 `permission_callback` / 1 `__return_true` / 1 `wp_ajax_` / 0 `admin_post_`); the one commit in range (R26's fix session) touched no route, gate or callback. Inventory: 17 `src/` directories, 20 frontend + 3 admin JS files, all with rows. — **R26:** grep re-run at `a33a9cc` and again after the fix session — reconciles exactly with R23–R25 (6 `register_rest_route` calls / 7 `permission_callback` / 1 `__return_true` / 1 `wp_ajax_` / 0 `admin_post_`); nothing in range touched a route, gate or callback. — **R25:** grep re-run at `612122e`, reconciles exactly with R23/R24's counts (6 `register_rest_route` calls / 7 `permission_callback` / 1 `__return_true` / 1 `wp_ajax_` / 0 `admin_post_` / 3 `rest_pre_serve_request` hits, 1 real); nothing in the 7-commit beta3 range touched a route, gate or permission callback. — **R24:** grep re-run at `788c8f2`, reconciles exactly with R23's counts (6 `register_rest_route` calls / 7 `permission_callback` / 1 `__return_true` / 1 `wp_ajax_` / 0 `admin_post_` / 3 `rest_pre_serve_request` hits of which 1 is real); nothing in range touched a route, gate or permission callback — the one production change is the order-received gate's internals, behind the same route rows. — **R23:** **No new route, AJAX handler, gate or permission callback**: the grep reconciles exactly (6 `register_rest_route` calls → 4 admin routes, 1 public GET, 2 guest beacons; 7 `permission_callback`; 1 `__return_true`; 1 `wp_ajax_`; 0 `admin_post_`; **3** `rest_pre_serve_request` hits of which **1** is a real `add_filter` and 2 are docblock mentions), and nothing in range touched one. **No new sink either.** For the **eighth** run running the range added something this table has no shape for — and this time it is not new surface at all but a **new blind spot in an existing check**: git-ignoring the scratch-worktree path (#176's fix) left a leftover worktree invisible to every stated integrity check, while this sweep's own sibling at `code-review.md:232` recurses into `.claude/` and reads the scratch copy as repository content (**#187**). The row-shape lesson now reads in five directions: what answers a request · what the project publishes for someone else to run (#150) · what writes the code the project publishes (#154) · which scopes the enforced set is assembled from (#162) · and **what the review process creates while running, then hides from itself**. — **R22 (2026-08-12):** **No new route, AJAX handler, gate or permission callback**: the grep reconciles exactly (4 admin REST routes, 1 admin AJAX, 2 guest beacons, 1 public GET, 1 `__return_true`, 0 `admin_post_`, 1 `rest_pre_serve_request`), unchanged from R21, and nothing in range touched one. **No new sink either.** For the **seventh** run running, what the range added has no row shape here — and this time it is the review system's **own isolation machinery**: `.claude/worktrees/`, created by the verifier-isolation fix that landed in this very range. It answers no request, ships nothing, and is not surface at all; what it *is* is a directory the same commit's tree-clean check then reported as dirty (**#176**), plus a harness that silently tests the wrong tree (**#177**). The row-shape lesson now reads in four directions: this sweep asks what answers a request; it must also ask what the project publishes for somebody else to run (#150), what writes the code the project publishes (#154), which permission scopes the enforced set is assembled from (#162), and **what the review process itself creates while running**. — **R21 (2026-08-12):** **No new route, AJAX handler, gate or permission callback**: the grep reconciles exactly (4 admin REST routes, 1 admin AJAX, 2 guest beacons, 1 public GET, 1 `__return_true`, 0 `admin_post_`, 1 `rest_pre_serve_request`), and neither `RestCors.php` nor `VisitorDataEndpoint.php` is in range. **No new sink either.** For the sixth run running the range added a shape this sweep has no term for, and this time it is not even in the repository: a **permission scope** — `~/.claude/settings.json`, user-scope, outside the worktree, applying to this project, never enumerated (**#162**). The row-shape lesson now reads in three directions: this sweep asks what answers a request; it must also ask what the project publishes for somebody else to run (#150), what writes the code the project publishes (#154), and **which scopes the enforced permission set is actually assembled from**. — **R20 (2026-08-11):** **No new route, AJAX handler, gate or permission callback** across R19's own fix session: the grep reconciles exactly (4 admin REST routes, 1 admin AJAX, 2 guest beacons, 1 public GET, 1 `__return_true`, 0 `admin_post_`, 1 `rest_pre_serve_request`), and `RestCors.php` / `VisitorDataEndpoint.php` are not in the range at all. **No new sink either** — and for the fifth run running, what the range *did* add has no term here. R19 met a **published artifact** (something the project distributes for a third party to run, #150); R20 meets its inverse: a **generator that writes OUR OWN shipped source from a remote input** (`tools/generate-phone-table.php`, **#154**, generalized as **PA-18**). It answers no request and ships nothing itself, yet every line it writes ships and executes on every install. Recorded here, and the row-shape lesson now reads in both directions: this sweep asks what answers a request; it must also ask **what the project publishes for somebody else to run**, and **what writes the code the project publishes**. — **R19 (2026-08-11):** **No new route, AJAX handler, gate or permission callback**, across the first range with real production code in it since R17: the grep reconciles exactly (4 admin REST routes, 1 admin AJAX, 2 guest beacons, 1 public GET, 1 `__return_true`, 0 `admin_post_`, 1 `rest_pre_serve_request`), and `RestCors.php` / `VisitorDataEndpoint.php` are not in the range at all. **No new sink either.** What the range added is a third category this sweep has no term for and neither does the Public Surface Inventory: a **published artifact** — the GTM container export in `.gtm-container-template/`, distributed for users to import into their *own* GTM account (**#150**). It answers no request and runs on no site, so it is neither surface nor sink, yet it can carry executable content (a `jsm` Custom JavaScript variable, an `html` Custom HTML tag) and the exporter's real account/container ids. Clean on both this run — 0 `jsm`, 0 `html`, ids zeroed, and outside `DIST_FILES` so it never ships — and it now has a Coverage Matrix row carrying those two questions. **Lesson, and it is this sweep's fifth of the same kind:** the grep asks what answers a request; it must also ask **what the project publishes for somebody else to run**. (2026-08-10 (R18): **No new route, AJAX handler, gate or permission callback** — the range holds no production code at all, and the grep reconciles exactly (4 admin REST routes, 1 admin AJAX, 2 guest beacons, 1 public GET, 1 `__return_true`, 0 `admin_post_`, 1 `rest_pre_serve_request`). (2026-08-10 (R17): **No new route, AJAX handler, gate or permission callback.** The grep reconciles exactly (4 admin REST routes, 1 admin AJAX, 2 guest beacons, 1 public GET, 1 `__return_true`, 0 `admin_post_`, 1 `rest_pre_serve_request`). `RestCors.php` **is** in range, but only its predicate body changed — the namespace comparison now lowercases the route, matching how `WP_REST_Server::match_request_to_handler()` resolves one; the gate itself is unchanged. **No new sink either**, which breaks a four-run streak of this sweep meeting a shape it had no term for. What it *did* meet is the inverse: an existing sink whose documented **levers** do not all work (#133/#134) — and this sweep asks what answers a request, never *what a control it names can actually be refused by*. That question belongs to whoever adds the control, which is why PA-16 now carries it. (2026-08-10 (R16): **No new route, AJAX handler, gate or permission callback** across a 43-commit range: the grep reconciles exactly (4 admin REST routes, 1 admin AJAX, 2 guest beacons, 1 public GET, 1 `__return_true`, 0 `admin_post_`, 1 `rest_pre_serve_request`), and `RestCors.php` / `VisitorDataEndpoint.php` are not in the range at all. **Two new sinks that answer no request**, so neither belongs in the Public Surface Inventory and both land here, which is the row-shape lesson of R13/R14/R15 holding for a fourth run: (1) `gtm4wpObserveMedia()` now injects `<script src>` at runtime for eight provider SDKs, moving them out of `wp_enqueue_script` - which also moved them out of `script_loader_tag`, where consent managers block third-party scripts (**#125**, fixed: a `gtm4wp_media_sdk_blocked` filter plus the per-provider script handles are the levers); (2) `DataLayer::queue_push()` accepts a JavaScript function name emitted **unquoted** into a `<script>` body, validated at the sink against the identifier grammar (correct - **#123** is about the predicate it *copied*, not about the check). (2026-08-05 (R15): **No new route, AJAX handler, gate or permission callback**: the grep reconciles exactly (4 admin REST routes, 1 admin AJAX, 2 guest beacons, 1 public GET, 1 `__return_true`, 0 `admin_post_`, 1 `rest_pre_serve_request`), and nothing in range touched one. New **file** with no row of its own: `jest.config.js` — and it needed none, because #101 had already widened the **Git hooks & CI** row's label to "every other tool config a hook or CI step feeds to an executable". That is the row-widening earning its keep: the previous three runs of this sweep each met a shape it had no term for (a `rest_pre_serve_request` filter, a namespace index route, a tool config), and this one landed inside a category that already existed. `js/admin/test-support/` is likewise not surface — referenced only from `jest.config.js`'s `moduleNameMapper`, imported by no production file, not a webpack entry, and outside `tools/build-release.js`'s `DIST_FILES` allow-list (verified, not assumed). (2026-08-05 (R14): **No new route, AJAX handler, gate or permission callback**: the grep reconciles exactly (4 admin REST routes, 1 admin AJAX, 2 guest beacons, 1 public GET, 1 `__return_true`, 0 `admin_post_`, 1 `rest_pre_serve_request`), and no file in range touched one. One new **sink** that is deliberately *not* an inventory row: `PageDataLayer::print_deferred_checkout_js()` takes the `print_script_block()` caller count from 16 to 17. It answers no request, so it belongs to the WooCommerce Module row — but note that this sweep found it only because the production diff was one commit long. **A sink is not surface and this sweep does not look for one**; the Duplication sweep's `wp_json_encode`/`print_script_block` ledger is what has to catch it, which is an argument for keeping that ledger honest (#111). But the sweep found new surface of a shape it has never had a term for, twice over. (1) **A namespace has a route the plugin never registered** — WordPress auto-registers the namespace index, and the #90/#97 CORS policy matched on the `gtm4wp/v2/` prefix, so `/gtm4wp/v2` sat outside the control named after the namespace (**#102**, fixed). Neither is a route, and the route-shaped grep set cannot see either. Lesson: this sweep asks *what answers a request*; it must also ask **what a namespace answers that nobody declared**, and **which files a hook or CI step hands to an executable**. Lesson: the grep finds things that *answer* requests; a filter that changes how every answer is *delivered* is surface too. ## Known Findings Log

Each finding is logged once. Status: `open` | `fixed` | `wontfix`.

> **Numbering note:** **#25, #26 and #27 were never allocated** — Report 3 ends at #24
> (its fix note says "all six findings #19-24") and Report 4 opens at #28. Nothing is
> missing; the counter simply skipped. Recorded here so the gap is not read as three
> lost findings by a future reader — or, worse, re-used.
>
> **To list open findings, extract the status column mechanically** (every row starts
> `| <n> | <sev> | <status> |`) rather than reading down the log. A status is a claim
> like any other in this system: #74 sat as "fixed in 2.0 only", carrying a disclosure
> embargo, for days after 1.22.5 had shipped the 1.x fix and the changelog caveat had
> been dropped on that basis.

> **Reports are local-only.** The detailed report files referenced below are git-ignored (see `.security/.gitignore`) because this is a public repo and reports carry exploit PoCs / possibly-unfixed detail. This log keeps only terse summaries — never paste a working payload or the full detail of an `open` Critical/High finding here.
>
> **Every report records `Reviewed at: <sha>`** — the HEAD commit it actually covered. The next review reads it to scope its diff (`<sha>..HEAD`, pre-review step 4) and to check cell staleness (step 5). Reports 1–5 predate the convention and record no sha; Report 6's was reconstructed from its coverage note, so **the next review's base is `ab7fa99`** (Review 6 reviewed up to `3a5a2a0`; `ab7fa99` is its own fix commit). Everything after `ab7fa99` — 14 commits as of 2026-07-17, including the whole `#398` cache-safe data-layer rewrite and the new public VisitorData endpoint — is **unreviewed**. *(Superseded: Report 7 reviewed `ab7fa99..e8f85e5` with fixes landing as `b7a7d33`; Report 8 reviewed `b7a7d33..54d12e5` with fixes as `a2b18c7`; Report 9 reviewed `a2b18c7..1ad1187` with fixes as `599fd7d`; Report 10 reviewed `599fd7d..7a607d2` and **never recorded its fix commit**, so Report 11 took `7a607d2` as its base and reviewed R10's whole fix session as part of its range; Report 11 reviewed `7a607d2..b58d427` and **also left its fix commit unrecorded**, so Report 12 took `b58d427` as its base and reviewed R11's three fix sessions as part of its range; Report 12 reviewed `b58d427..58d2f8f` and **also left its fix commit unrecorded**, so Report 13 took `58d2f8f` as its base and reviewed R12's whole fix session as part of its range; Report 13 reviewed `58d2f8f..132e7ca` with fixes as `d38990b`, correctly recorded; Report 14 reviewed `d38990b..4ce7537` **plus `d38990b` itself**, which R13's entry had handed forward to be read deliberately rather than inherited, and **left its own `Fixes:` unfilled** — so Report 15 took `4ce7537` as its base and reviewed R14's whole six-finding fix session (`b2bd170`) as part of its range. Report 15 reviewed `4ce7537..80e116b` with fixes as **`707a3a1`, recorded at commit time** — the first time in six reviews this line was filled in without a later run having to reconstruct it. Report 16 reviewed `707a3a1..1b4445f` - 43 commits, the largest range this system has covered - with fixes as **`b8448cb`, recorded at commit time**, which is the second review running that this line was filled in without a later run having to reconstruct it. Report 17 reviewed `b8448cb..2ea4441` — 4 commits, and per the standing note it read `b8448cb`'s own production code deliberately, which is where **all eight** of its findings came from — with fixes as **`ddf9049`, recorded at commit time**, the third review running that this line was filled in without a later run having to reconstruct it. Report 18 reviewed `ddf9049..64612e0` — 1 commit and no production code at all, so it ran as an empty-diff deep pass, and **three of its four findings came from reading `ddf9049` itself**, which is the fourth review running that the standing note has paid for itself. Report 19 reviewed `ffd2a63..6fe789c` — 5 commits — **plus `ffd2a63` itself**, per the standing note, which is where **two of its eight findings** came from; that is the fifth review running. Note that HEAD moved *during* R19 (`6fe789c`, a `.upstream/` doc commit, landed after the run's first `rev-parse`), so the recorded sha is the later one — re-derive HEAD before writing this line rather than trusting the one you read at the start. **The base for the next review is R19's fix commit; if that line is left unfilled, the base is `6fe789c`.**)*
>
> **This has now happened four reviews running (R10, R11, R12, R13).** Each time the unfilled
> `Fixes:` line was harmless — better than harmless, it is why #67/#72 (R10), #82–#85
> (R11) and #91/#94/#97 (R12) were found at all, since fix-session code is otherwise
> reviewed by nobody. Treat the pattern as evidence for the rule two paragraphs down
> rather than as bookkeeping to tighten: **when the previous entry has no fix sha, take
> the recorded `Reviewed at:` and let the fixes be reviewed.** R13 is the clearest case
> yet — five of its seven findings sit in fix-session code or in the toolchain change
> that shipped beside it, none of which any review would have covered otherwise.
>
> **A `Fixes: pending` that is never filled in is not a gap — it is a base.** R10 left its fix commit unrecorded. Read literally, that makes the next review's base R10's own HEAD, which pulls the entire fix session into the range instead of hiding it under one. R11 did exactly that and got #82–#85 out of it. So: record the fix commit when you land it, and when the previous entry has no sha, do **not** reach for the last commit that looks like a fix — take the recorded `Reviewed at:` and let the fixes be reviewed.
>
> **A fix commit is a review's base, and therefore never gets reviewed by default.** Report 10 found #67 and #72 by deliberately reading the production code of `599fd7d` — its own base, written during R9's fix session and only ever self-checked. Every fix commit has this property. When the next review's base is a fix commit, read it.
>
> **An empty production diff is not a reason to stop (step 9).** Reports 9 and 10 are the worked examples. R9's range contained no production code, and the deep pass produced 11 findings including 2 Medium — two structurally invisible to every prior review: **#55** because the exposure lens asks "should this value be here at all?", which no escaping check ever answers, and **#56** because it is a silent wrong *value*, not an unsafe one. R10's range contained one version-bump line and still produced 8, because it stopped trusting the matrix dates and re-derived staleness from `git log` — see **#73**. Note also what the mechanical sweeps could **not** see on their own: #58 (a dead *branch*, not a dead symbol), #59 (a global read that *is* gated but still emits a placeholder) and #70 (a statement that runs and does nothing) each needed the sweep's litmus sharpened, not just re-run.

### Report 1: `.security/code-review-report-2026-07-10-1501.md`

Reflected/stored XSS review of every path where HTML/`<script>` output depends on URL/request/header input. All findings share one root cause (`print_script_block()` decoding HTML entities) and were fixed in the working tree the same session, with two regression tests.

| # | Sev | Status | Summary | File(s) |
|---|-----|--------|---------|---------|
| 1 | Critical | fixed | Reflected XSS — `?s=` search term (`siteSearchTerm`) breaks out of the dataLayer JS string; `get_search_query()` returns `esc_attr`'d `&quot;` which `JSON_HEX_TAG` cannot catch and `print_script_block()`'s decode resurrects into a raw `"`. Fixed by adding `JSON_HEX_AMP\|JSON_HEX_QUOT\|JSON_HEX_APOS`. | `src/Modules/PageVariables/PageVariablesModule.php`, `src/Frontend/ContainerCode.php` |
| 2 | High | fixed | Root cause — `ScriptTag::print_script_block()` ran a blanket `htmlspecialchars_decode()` after `wp_kses`, resurrecting `&quot;`/`&lt;`/`&#039;` from any `esc_js`/`esc_attr`-escaped value into break-out characters. Reworked to restore only the ampersand (`str_replace('&amp;','&', …)`). | `src/Frontend/ScriptTag.php` |
| 3 | High | fixed | Stored XSS — WooCommerce purchase dataLayer: `esc_js`'d order/billing fields (e.g. a billing name containing a double quote, entered at checkout) break out via the same decode. Fixed by the hex flags on the purchase `wp_json_encode` + the print_script_block root-cause fix. | `src/Modules/WooCommerce/PurchaseTracking.php`, `src/Modules/WooCommerce/ProductData.php` |
| 4 | Medium | fixed | `geoCloudflareCountryCode` from the spoofable `HTTP_CF_IPCOUNTRY` header follows the same `esc_js` → decode break-out class. Covered by the two fixes above. | `src/Modules/PageVariables/PageVariablesModule.php` |
| 5 | Low | fixed | `siteSearchFrom` from `HTTP_REFERER` — lower risk (`esc_url_raw` strips `"`) but same class; covered by the hex-flag + amp-only-restore fixes. | `src/Modules/PageVariables/PageVariablesModule.php` |
| 6 | Low | fixed | `esc_js`'d values embedded directly in hardcoded `<script>` strings (data layer name, disabled user role) were also resurrected by the old decode. Now inert after the amp-only restore (RI-3). | `src/Frontend/ContainerCode.php` |

**Defense-in-depth flags** also added to `src/Frontend/DataLayer.php` (additional pushes) for uniformity, though that path (`wp_add_inline_script`) was not exploitable.

**Regression tests:** `tests/unit/Frontend/ScriptTagTest::test_print_script_block_does_not_decode_quote_and_tag_entities`, `tests/unit/Frontend/ContainerCodeTest::test_header_begin_does_not_decode_html_entities_in_datalayer_values`. Full suite: 140 tests green; `vendor/bin/phpcs` clean (bar the pre-existing `$echo` warnings, FP-3).

### Report 2: `.security/code-review-report-2026-07-10-1606.md`

First full pass over the previously-unreviewed component groups (Admin/REST/Settings, Options/Field, all non-PageVariables modules, bootstrap/compat, WooCommerce Cplx/Perf/Types) + all six whole-repo sweeps. **No Critical/High.** Findings 7-8 are the notable ones (both consequences of the Review-1 fix); 9-18 are dead-code/consistency/quality.

| # | Sev | Status | Summary | File(s) |
|---|-----|--------|---------|---------|
| 7 | Medium | fixed | Defense-in-depth (RI-2): the `wc_enqueue_js` checkout-products sink emitted `wp_json_encode` output into a raw `<script>` without hex flags. Added the full hex-flag set (now matches every sibling sink). | `src/Modules/WooCommerce/PageDataLayer.php` |
| 8 | Medium | fixed | Data-quality (RI-4): `get_raw_order_datalayer()` `esc_js`-pre-escaped ~30 order fields, corrupting data post-Review-1. Removed the `esc_js`; values now reach the hex-flag `wp_json_encode` raw. Regression test added (`ProductDataTest::test_raw_order_datalayer_passes_values_without_entity_escaping`). | `src/Modules/WooCommerce/ProductData.php` |
| 9 | Low | fixed | Dead JS: `gtm4wp-html5media.js` was built but never enqueued. Wired into MediaEvents as an opt-in tracker (new option `event-html5-media`, default off; constant `GTM4WP_OPTION_EVENTS_HTML5MEDIA`). | `js/frontend/gtm4wp-html5media.js`, `src/Modules/MediaEvents/*`, `compat/constants.php` |

### Report 4: `.security/code-review-report-2026-07-14-1252.md`

Review of the 9 commits in `d36bca3..b15b034` (native Axeptio CMP integration, Contact Form 7 modernization, AMP amp-wp 2.x modernization, WebToffee deprecation, `sandboxedScripts` blacklist group class, `ContainerCode` `$echo` cleanup). **No Critical/High/Medium.** Every new `<script>`/dataLayer sink conforms to the established hardening (full hex flags, sanitize-on-save, output-sink allow-list, delegated AMP sink, React escaping). Three Low observations only.

| # | Sev | Status | Summary | File(s) |
|---|-----|--------|---------|---------|
| 28 | Low | fixed | Consistency/robustness: the CF7 tracker attaches `document`-level listeners in a bare IIFE with no double-init guard (unlike the media-tracker family, PA-9/#22); a twice-injected bundle would double-push. Fixed with a `window.gtm4wp_cf7_inited` guard; regression test `contact-form-7-tracker.test.js` "does not re-register … when the bundle loads twice". | `js/frontend/gtm4wp-contact-form-7-tracker.js` |
| 29 | Low | fixed | Latent drift: `ContainerCode::header_top` printed inline JS via raw `wp_kses()` (no ampersand-restore) vs `print_script_block()`. Fixed by routing `header_top` through `print_script_block()` (same path as `header_begin`), so head-block consent JS gets the ampersand restored (and the `type` attr is preserved on non-HTML5 themes). Regression test `ContainerCodeTest::test_header_top_restores_ampersands_in_head_block_js`. | `src/Frontend/ContainerCode.php` |
| 30 | Low | wontfix | Privacy-by-default (informational): CF7 `integrate-wpcf7-inputs` defaults to `full`, pushing submitted field values (potential PII) into the dataLayer. **Maintainer: expected behavior** — documented, opt-out provided (`names`/`none`), 1.x-compatible default. Not a vulnerability. | `src/Modules/ContactForm7/AdminSchema.php` |

**Fix session (2026-07-14):** #28 and #29 **fixed** (both with regression tests); #30 closed **wontfix** (intended, documented behavior). #29 routed `header_top` through `print_script_block()` — the same sanitizer as `header_begin` — resolving the ampersand-restore drift for all head-block consent JS and preserving the `<script>` `type` attribute on non-HTML5 themes. Verified: **PHP 282 tests green** (+1), **JS 161 tests / 17 suites green** (+11, new `contact-form-7-tracker.test.js`), `npm run build` + `npm run lint:js` clean, `phpcs` clean (bar the blessed unused-mock-param warning). CHANGELOG updated.

**Verification (no findings):** Axeptio settings sink RI-2-compliant with a hostile-input regression test (`ConsentModeAxeptioTest::test_head_js_hex_encodes_breakout_characters_in_settings`, both-directions); data-layer name validated to a JS identifier so unquoted `window.<name>` is safe; CF7 config SELECT-restricted + hex-flagged via `wp_add_inline_script`; Blacklist `sandboxedScripts` allow-list-validated at the sink AND on save; AMP delegated sink (FP-4) with `rawurlencode`d ID; admin JS React-escaped with `encodeURIComponent`'d fetch URL; standalone Axeptio module removed with no dangling refs. Baseline **281 tests green**, `phpcs` clean.

Fixed with a `delete item.internal_id` in `gtm4wp_blocks_to_crosssell_item()`; regression test "strips internal_id from cross-sell view_item_list / select_item items". | `js/frontend/gtm4wp-woocommerce-blocks.js` |

### Report 6: `.security/code-review-report-2026-07-15-2046.md`

**Reviewed at:** `3a5a2a0` · **Fixes landed:** `ab7fa99` · **Base for the next review:** `ab7fa99`

Review of the two production features since Review 5 (`07117bc..HEAD`): **settings export/import (#87)** and the **container output kill switch (#413)**. **No Critical/High/Medium.** Import/export is a strict subset of the already-reviewed save path (schema-driven re-sanitization, `json_decode`-only, size/depth caps, envelope validation, defaults-rebuilt row, capability + `wp_rest` nonce); kill switch is static-string output + a `PHASE_EXPERIMENTAL` checkbox mirroring placement-OFF. One Low.

| # | Sev | Status | Summary | File(s) |
|---|-----|--------|---------|---------|
| 32 | Low | fixed | Robustness/consistency: the settings-import path decodes the raw payload itself and hands values straight to `Field::sanitize()`, skipping the REST-schema per-field type layer the save route applies. A crafted import file supplying an array where a scalar field is expected triggered a PHP `Array to string conversion` warning (value collapsed to `"Array"`). Admin-only (`manage_options`), not a security vulnerability — sanitizers still reduce to safe forms and every output sink re-escapes. Fixed by a `Field::to_string()` helper (`is_scalar($v) ? (string) $v : ''`) used by every scalar cast (SELECT/TEXTAREA/TEXT/default + the MULTISELECT and TABLE cell casts), so a non-scalar collapses to `''` with no warning and both entry points behave identically. Regression tests: `FieldTest::test_scalar_sanitizers_handle_non_scalar_input_without_warning` + `::test_multiselect_and_table_handle_nested_arrays_without_warning`. | `src/Options/Field.php` |

**Fix session (2026-07-15):** #32 **fixed** — added the type-defensive `Field::to_string()` and routed every scalar `(string)` cast in `Field::sanitize()` through it, with two `FieldTest` regression tests asserting no PHP warning + a safe result (scalar fields and nested multiselect/table cells). Verified: **PHP 439 tests / 1429 assertions green** (+2), `phpcs` clean on both changed files (only the pre-existing `$default` reserved-keyword warning on the `make_field` test helper remains). No `js/` changed, so no `build/` regeneration. CHANGELOG updated (`* Fixed:` import type-normalization bullet).

### Report 7: `.security/code-review-report-2026-07-17-1303.md`

**Reviewed at:** `e8f85e5` · **Base:** `ab7fa99` (range `ab7fa99..HEAD`, 15 commits) · **Fixes:** applied in the working tree, commit pending — the fix commit is the base for the next review.

First review of the **VisitorData module + its A0-reachable `__return_true` `gtm4wp/v2/visitor-data` route** (`#398` cache-safe data layer), plus the `#3` MutationObserver media work, `#324` source-aware filter, and `#220`/`#258` PageVariables changes. Identity gates hold (no IDOR, no XSS, no PII exposure — the `#398` exposure work is sound), but the "read-only GET" claim was false (it mutated WC session state) and Phase 3's one-shots never fired (no WC session on REST). **No Critical.** 13 findings. A recurring theme: three of the four most serious were invisible to a green suite because a test double was more capable than the real collaborator (see test-review patterns).

| # | Sev | Status | Summary | File(s) |
|---|-----|--------|---------|---------|
| 33 | High | fixed | WC session is not initialized on a custom REST route, so the two cache-safe one-shot events (re-add `add_to_cart`, reliable-purchase fallback) silently never fired — and were also omitted from the cached HTML, so the events were lost outright. Fixed by loading the WC session for the request, gated on the one-shot event cookie so an ordinary anonymous read pays no WC cost. Regression test does not stub `WC()` wholesale. | `src/Modules/WooCommerce/PageDataLayer.php`, `WooCommerceModule.php` |
| 34 | Medium (security, A0) | fixed | State change on an unauthenticated GET (PA-1/FP-5 cond. 3): the public read-only session endpoint consumed WC session markers while resolving, so a cross-site top-level navigation (Lax cookies) could destroy a visitor's pending purchase/add_to_cart event. Fixed: the GET is genuinely read-only; marker consumption + the `_ga_tracked` write happen only on the authenticated POST beacon (extended to cover the re-add). Read-only property is test-pinned. | `src/Modules/WooCommerce/PageDataLayer.php`, `WooCommerceModule.php` |
| 35 | Medium | fixed | A time-limited `wp_rest` nonce baked into cacheable HTML 403s once stale (WP rejects an anonymous stale-nonce request before `permission_callback`), silently dropping Tier-2 data + the confirm beacon on long-TTL cached pages. Fixed: no nonce on the anonymous read (it needs none); the endpoint returns a fresh nonce that authenticates the beacon. | `src/Modules/VisitorData/VisitorDataEndpoint.php`, `VisitorDataModule.php`, `js/frontend/gtm4wp-visitor-data.js` |
| 36 | Low | fixed | Stale admin-facing option description (claimed the one-shots were still omitted). | `src/Modules/VisitorData/AdminSchema.php` |
| 37 | Low | fixed | Correctness: a post with a single PublishPress **guest** author fell to the `get_userdata()` branch, reporting the creating WP user's name. Gate changed to `>= 1`; arrays still gated `> 1`. | `src/Modules/PageVariables/PageVariablesModule.php` |
| 38 | Low | fixed | Improvement: `apply_filters_deprecated( gtm4wp_eec_product_array )` emits the notice per product; gated to once per request via an instance flag, `apply_filters()` thereafter. | `src/Modules/WooCommerce/ProductData.php` |
| 39 | Low | fixed | Nit: the "no per-line source" contract passed an empty array instead of `null` when called with one arg. | `src/Modules/WooCommerce/ListTracking.php` |
| 40 | High | fixed | Unbounded loop / tab hang: the `#3` shared MutationObserver marked the element an SDK (Spotify) then replaces, so the replacement was re-wired forever. Fixed: the wired-marker now follows a replaced element (provider-agnostic). Test double now performs the real `replaceChild`. | `js/frontend/lib/native-video-params.js`, `gtm4wp-spotify.js` |
| 41 | Low | fixed | Correctness: Twitch container-id counter (module-scoped) reset on bundle re-injection → id collision. Moved to `window` scope. | `js/frontend/gtm4wp-twitch.js` |
| 42 | Low | fixed | Correctness (pre-existing): `gtm4wp-html5media.js` lacked the family's zero-duration guard, firing every milestone at once. Adopted `if ( ! duration ) return;`. | `js/frontend/gtm4wp-html5media.js` |
| 43 | Low | fixed | Correctness (was open/unverified): PublishPress author read via `isset()` blanks the value if the object exposes it through `__get()` without `__isset()`. Now read through `__get()` (guarded by `property_exists`/`method_exists`). Regression test uses a magic-`__get`-without-`__isset` stub. | `src/Modules/PageVariables/PageVariablesModule.php` |
| 44 | Low (security, A0) | fixed | Cache hygiene (was open/low-conf): the login-gate cookie **clear** runs on an anonymous request whose response could be full-page cached, replaying the `Set-Cookie` to others. Now marks that response no-store (`nocache_headers()`). | `src/Modules/VisitorData/VisitorDataModule.php` |
| 45 | Low | fixed (by #40) | Correctness (was open): Dailymotion shares the #40 element-replacement class; the provider-agnostic re-mark (#40) already covers it whether `DM.player` replaces the element or uses it in place. No separate change; covered by the generic guard + its test. | `js/frontend/gtm4wp-dailymotion.js` |

**Fix session (2026-07-17):** all 13 findings resolved (#45 by #40, no separate change). Fixes landed across two batches (the #33/#34/#35/#40 cluster, then the #36–#39/#41–#43/#44 Low set) each with regression tests that fail without the fix — except #38, whose once-per-request notice is not unit-observable (Brain Monkey routes `apply_filters_deprecated` and `apply_filters` identically), so its test guards that the deprecated filter keeps applying to every product. Verified: **PHP 535 tests / 1776 assertions green**, **JS 288 tests / 22 suites green**, `phpcs` 0 errors (blessed BE-2 warnings only), `npm run build` + `lint:js` clean. CHANGELOG updated. The report's former "Open questions" tier was promoted to findings #43–#45 (tracked, not dropped) per the maintainer's exhaustive-findings preference — now codified in the command + agent so nothing is exiled to a separate tier.

### Report 9: `.security/code-review-report-2026-07-22-1433.md`

**Reviewed at:** `1ad1187` · **Base:** `a2b18c7` (range `a2b18c7..1ad1187`, 2 commits, **no production code**) · **Fixes landed:** `599fd7d` — **the base for the next review is `599fd7d`**.

The first **empty-diff escalation** run (pre-review step 9): with nothing new to diff, this became the deep pass — all 7 whole-repo sweeps re-run, the oldest-reviewed `[x]` groups (2026-07-10) and every script-sink / order-data / request-header component re-audited against the current patterns catalog, and the ⭐ patterns re-verified at their call sites. **No Critical/High**, and **no regression** on any prior fix (#34 read-only GET, #35/PA-12 nonce, RI-2 flags, RI-3 no-decode, PA-1/PA-10 gates all hold). 11 findings, all pre-existing; per the disclosure rule these are one-line summaries only — none is reachable by an actor below A4 except #55, which is an exposure/product decision with no payload.

| # | Sev | Status | Summary | File(s) |
|---|-----|--------|---------|---------|
| 55 | Medium (security, A0 reads / A2-A3 data) | fixed | Exposure (RI-11): the "Post Terms" option also publishes **every non-`_`-prefixed post meta value** into the anonymous-readable data layer, while its label and description mention only taxonomy values. Correctly escaped — the gap is the admin's informed consent (unprotected meta is where ACF and many plugins keep field values). Off by default; per-key opt-out exists via the `gtm4wp_post_meta_in_datalayer` filter. Recommend splitting the meta dump into its own opt-in field, or at minimum stating it in the description. | `src/Modules/PageVariables/PageVariablesModule.php`, `src/Modules/PageVariables/AdminSchema.php` |

Both variables are new in 2.0. Fix with a UTF-8-aware split; changelog = edit the existing `* Added:` bullet (unreleased feature). | `src/Modules/PageVariables/PageVariablesModule.php` |
| 57 | Low | fixed | Correctness + PA-4 + duplication: the `gtm4wp_orderid_tracked` de-dupe key has three incompatible definitions — `esc_js`'d order number in a raw `<script>` body (PHP), raw order number (JS runtime), and an int order **id** (server-side cookie read). They coincide only on a default install where the order number is the numeric id, so a store using prefixed/sequential order numbers loses the cross-path guard. Not XSS. Probe-verified. Also, the filter-supplied variable *name* is `esc_js`'d but never validated as a JS identifier, so a malformed name is a `SyntaxError` that kills the whole head block. | `src/Frontend/ContainerCode.php` |

**Fix session (2026-07-22):** all 11 findings resolved. Two carried a maintainer decision that shaped the fix:

- **#55** was split into two options rather than only re-worded, **with a migration path**: `GTM4WP_OPTION_INCLUDE_POSTMETA` is new, and `Migration::seed_post_meta_option()` seeds it from the legacy combined option so a site that was already publishing custom fields keeps an identical `pagePostTerms` payload after upgrading. The seeding is self-guarded on key absence (it runs every admin request like the container-row seeding, can never run twice, and never overrides an admin's own later choice). The new field's description states plainly what it publishes and names the opt-out filter.
- **#60** gained an operator-facing signal on top of the validation: a silently discarded wp-config constant is very hard to diagnose, so `Options::hardcoded_errors()` records every rejected `GTM4WP_HARDCODED_*` constant and `Notices` renders a non-dismissible admin warning **naming the constant**. The container ID was already validated; the two environment constants are now consistent with it, and re-validated at the output sink (PA-2).

Notable during the fix: the existing `ContainerCodeTest::test_header_top_renders_global_vars_from_filter` **asserted the #58 bug** (`const nullVar = false;` with a comment calling it "1.x parity"), so the fix required correcting the test's expectation — a reminder that a green suite can pin a defect. #57's guard output turned out byte-identical for ordinary numeric order numbers (the JSON encoder supplies the quotes `esc_js` needed manually), which is why nothing broke and why the regression test uses an order number containing `&`.

Verified: **PHP 588 tests / 2060 assertions green** (+33 tests), **JS 296 tests / 23 suites green** (+1), `phpcs` **0 errors repo-wide** (blessed unused-mock-param warnings only), `npm run build` + `lint:js` clean. CHANGELOG + readme.txt updated together: new `* Added:` for the custom-fields option (with the "nothing changes for an existing site" note), `* Changed:` for the wp-config validation, `* Fixed:` for the order-number dedupe and the global-vars types; the word-count bullet was **edited** rather than given a `* Fixed:` entry, per the unreleased-feature rule, and the null-global bullet was extended to the author archive.

**Verification (no findings):** hex flags intact on all 11 script-context sinks and 0 `htmlspecialchars_decode` occurrences; the public GET still performs no session/meta write and its two POST beacons still resolve every id from the session (FP-5 conditions 1–3, no IDOR); the anonymous read still sends no nonce and the beacons adopt the endpoint's fresh one (PA-12); all 5 admin mutations carry nonce **and** capability, with the notice id allow-listed; container ID re-validated at both output sinks; all 24 superglobal reads unslashed + validated; order PII is A1-to-own-data behind the order-key/session gate; zero `$wpdb` and zero order `get_post_meta()`; blacklist entities allow-listed at the sink; no new surface. The one non-production commit in range (triage-workflow hardening) correctly closes the redirect leg of R8's #50 by enforcing the host allow-list on every redirect hop. Baseline **PHP 555 tests / 1982 assertions green**, `phpcs` **0 errors** repo-wide — identical to R8, confirming the empty production diff.

### Report 10: `.security/code-review-report-2026-07-28-2113.md`

**Reviewed at:** `7a607d2` · **Base:** `599fd7d` (range `599fd7d..HEAD`, 30 commits, **one production line-pair: the `2.0.0-dev` → `2.0.0-beta1` version bump**) · **Fixes:** pending — record the fix commit here; it is the base for the next review.

The **second consecutive empty-diff escalation**. Everything in range other than the version bump is devDependency bumps + npm `overrides`, CI branch repointing, `.gitignore`, and `.claude/` docs, so the deep pass replaced the short one again. **No Critical/High/Medium; 8 Low (#66–#73), none a security finding** — no finding has an actor-reachable path from request input to a script/HTML sink or to another actor's data, so Actor/Conf. are non-security throughout.

Two things shaped the yield. First, the pass was steered by **actual file mtimes instead of the recorded matrix dates**, which immediately exposed #73: the Frontend JS and Admin JS rows read `[x] 2026-07-15` while 18 of those files had changed on 07-16/18/22 and should have read `[~]`. Five findings live in exactly the files R9 deprioritized on the strength of that stale date. Second, this review read the **R9 fix commit's own production code** — which is this review's *base*, and had therefore never been independently reviewed, only self-checked during the fix session; #67 and #72 both come from there.

On a site using the supported data-layer rename option the push is silently skipped by its own truthiness guard — or lands in another vendor's array. | `js/frontend/gtm4wp-woocommerce.js` |
| 67 | Low | fixed | Correctness (probe-verified): the X-Forwarded-For branch splits on `,` with no `trim()`, but XFF is comma+**space** separated, so every entry after the first fails `filter_var` and the loop only ever works for entry 1. Direct sibling of #62 in the same function. Fails conservatively (falls back to `REMOTE_ADDR`), so no security consequence. | `src/Frontend/VisitorIp.php` |
| 68 | Low | fixed | Correctness + doc accuracy (probe-verified, same class as #65): the list-attribution cookie's cap comment says "evict oldest", but the keys are product ids and `Object.keys()` orders integer-like keys ascending — so eviction drops the **lowest product id**, not the oldest entry. The DoS bound still holds; the stated reason does not. | `js/frontend/gtm4wp-ecommerce-generic.js` |
| 69 | Low | fixed | Data quality (probe-verified): a product form with no quantity input yields `quantity: null` / `value: 0` on `add_to_cart`, because the intended `\|\| 1` fallback is gated on `isNaN( null )`, which is false. | 71 | Low | fixed | Consistency (PA-9): two bundles attach `document`-level listeners from a bare module body with no double-init guard, so a twice-injected bundle double-pushes every ecommerce/form event. **Verification (no findings):** 30 `wp_json_encode` sites enumerated — all 11 script-context sinks carry the full hex flags, the 2 flagless ones are matrix-blessed (numeric-only value; user-meta write), the 6 `ListTracking` ones are attribute-context [**corrected by R11: there are 7 — see #87**]; 0 entity-decode calls; all 24 superglobal reads unslashed + validated; ABSPATH guard on every `src/`+`compat/` file (mechanical, zero misses); zero `$wpdb`, zero `unserialize()`, zero order `get_post_meta()`; all 5 admin mutations nonce **and** capability with the notice id allow-listed; the A0 GET re-read in full with no session/cookie/meta write and a fresh nonce for the beacons (#34/#35 hold); #57's single-spelling order-tracked key holds end to end; RI-13 both passes clean; R9's `Options`/`Migration`/`Notices` fix code correct and self-guarded; Axeptio's admin fetch is host-hardcoded with `encodeURIComponent`, and remote values render as React-escaped text, never `RawHTML` (PA-13 holds).
