### External scan: 2026-07-29 (no `/code-review` run)

**Scanned at:** `7a607d2` (same HEAD as Report 10) · **Source:** an independent multi-agent scan of the whole repository, run outside this system; six findings survived its verification panel, five are recorded here (two of its findings describe one allowlist entry from different angles and are logged once, as #76). Detail lives in the local scan report directory, not here.

The value of this entry is not the findings, it is **why this system could not have produced them**. Every one sits in code the Coverage Matrix marks `[x]`, so none is a coverage gap:

- **A frozen verdict (#74).** PA-7 carried "not XSS" from 2026-07-10, derived at one call site and generalized. Severity actually depends on what the *matched* text contains, which the entry never asked. A pattern that records a conclusion stops the next reviewer from re-deriving it.
- **A miscounted family (#74 again).** The entry named "two injectors"; there were four. The two unguarded ones reached `preg_replace` through a helper one file away, so the module-scoped grep returned only the fixed ones.
- **A waiver nobody re-derived (#78).** FP-5 condition 3 read "it keeps a nonce", and one is verified — but not one that binds a guest caller. PA-12 already held the missing fact; the two entries never met, and "do not re-flag" closed the question for 13 review-days.
- **A scope boundary (#76, #77).** `.claude/`, `.githooks/` and `.github/` had no matrix row and no inventory grep, so they were absent rather than unreviewed — the failure mode this file warns about in bold, applied to a category the warning did not anticipate.
- **A missing lens (#75).** RI-6 asks whether request input is *sanitized*; nothing asked whether it is *authentic*. The code passes every existing check.

| # | Sev | Status | Summary | File(s) |
|---|-----|--------|---------|---------|
| 74 | Medium | **fixed in both lines** (2.0 + 1.x; status corrected 2026-08-04) | XSS (PA-7/RI-17): a data-bearing `preg_replace` replacement undid an escape that had already run. Fixed by removing the regex from the shared helper (literal `strpos`/`substr_replace`), which covers both unguarded call sites; 3 regression tests. **Was recorded as "fixed in 2.0 only" with an active disclosure embargo. R11 re-derived that against the branch rather than reading the status: `origin/1.x` carries the patched helper (`85e29b9`), shipped as 1.22.5, so the embargo ended and `b0cddc2` correctly dropped the caveat from the changelog — but this row still said the other line was unpatched.** A disclosure-bearing status is exactly the kind that must be re-checked against the code, not inherited. | `src/Modules/WooCommerce/Helpers.php`, `src/Modules/WooCommerce/ListTracking.php` |
| 75 | Low | **fixed** 2026-08-04 | Input authenticity (RI-18): the X-Forwarded-For branch scans left-to-right and returns the first public entry, but an appending proxy puts the address it observed on the right — so the value reported is the one the client chose. Analytics integrity only; nothing here makes an access decision on it. Demonstrated live 2026-07-30. **Fixed with a trusted-proxy allow-list option** (`GTM4WP_OPTION_INCLUDE_VISITOR_IP_PROXIES`): with it set, an XFF list is walked from the right skipping the operator's own hops, and a single-value header is honored only when `REMOTE_ADDR` is itself a declared proxy. Empty list = the previous reading, unchanged, plus a non-dismissible admin notice — the informed-consent half, since both states look identical on the settings screen. See the scoping note below for the part that was NOT in the original analysis. | `src/Frontend/VisitorIp.php`, `src/Modules/PageVariables/AdminSchema.php`, `src/Admin/Notices.php`, `compat/functions.php` |
| 89 | Low | **fixed** 2026-08-04 | PA-2 (found while fixing #75, not by a review pass): the custom-IP-header allow-list regex was **unanchored on the SAVE side** — the exact defect #62 fixed on the read side, in the sibling entry point, left behind for two reviews. It accepted any string containing one allowed character and stored the mangled result; the reader's anchored check then rejected it again, so nothing unsafe reached a sink and the only cost was an admin seeing a malformed value accepted and silently ignored. Both ends now share one anchored rule. | `src/Modules/PageVariables/AdminSchema.php` |
| 76 | Medium | **fixed** 2026-08-04 | Toolchain (PA-14, D0→D1): a pre-approved tool permission was broader than the write surface the skill it serves documents, so third-party text ingested by the triage workflows could drive an unattended authenticated write. Prose guards in the command file were the only countermeasure and are not enforcement. **Fixed by removing the entry**: prefix matching cannot express "read verbs only" (a write flag can appear anywhere in the command), so narrowing was not available and removal is the fix — reads and writes now both prompt, which is what the skill's draft-until-approved contract implies anyway. Lives in git-ignored local config, so no diff will ever show it **and the fix does not propagate to another checkout**. | `.claude/settings.local.json` |
| 77 | ~~Low~~ **Medium** (re-rated 2026-08-04) | **fixed 2026-08-12 on this clone — class deliberately still open** | Toolchain (PA-14, D0→D1): the commit-msg hook executed a script resolved from the checked-out worktree, so reviewing an untrusted PR branch locally ran that branch's shell code as the maintainer — and the `Stop` hook did the same on every Claude turn, which was the higher-frequency half and had never been stated. **Fixed by moving the entry point out of the tree and reading the script from a fixed ref** (`git show master:…`), with the runner failing **closed**. Verified by measurement in a scratch repo, not by reading: an out-of-tree `core.hooksPath` fires correctly, a branch replacing either tracked hook file no longer changes what runs, `--no-verify` still escapes, enforcement and the `[skip changelog]` hatch both still work, and stdin survives for the stop mode. **The obvious one-liner was measured to fail OPEN** and was replaced. **Scope, stated rather than implied:** this closes the *unattended* trigger only — `composer phpcs`, `npm test` and `vendor/bin/phpunit` still execute branch-supplied config, which `tools/check-branch.js` prompts about, so the class stays open by design. It is **local git config, so it protects one clone and propagates to none**; deliberately not wired to a `package.json` `prepare` script, since that script is worktree-supplied too and would be the branch installing its own defence. An earlier attempt (recorded in the 2026-08-10 external scan) was declined because it blocked every commit until an installer had run; this one changes **no tracked file** to take effect. | `~/.githooks/gtm4wp/` (out of tree), `.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/skills/changelog/SKILL.md` |
| 78 | Medium | **fixed** 2026-08-04 | Authorization strength (FP-5 cond. 3): the guest `confirm-*-tracked` beacons verify a `wp_rest` nonce, which for logged-out callers is a site-wide constant per tick — and the plugin publishes it from its own public GET. The token binds no particular caller, so it filters malformed requests rather than authorizing. Effect is analytics integrity (a purchase event permanently suppressed, `_ga_tracked` written), not disclosure. FP-5 conditions 1 and 2 still hold. **Fixed with an Origin/Referer check** in the permission callback; the nonce stays as a malformed-request filter with a comment saying so. **The obvious fix does not work and the code now records why** — see #90. | `src/Modules/WooCommerce/PageDataLayer.php` |
| 90 | Medium | **fixed** 2026-08-04 | Cross-origin exposure of the plugin's own REST namespace, found while verifying #78's fix rather than by any lens in this system. WordPress registers `rest_send_cors_headers()` on `rest_pre_serve_request` **by default**, and it reflects the request `Origin` and sends `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true` — so any third-party page can read these routes' responses with the visitor's cookies attached. Two consequences: a visitor's WC-session-derived data is readable cross-origin (browser `SameSite` defaults probably block it today, but that is a browser default, not a control this plugin owns), and **any token these routes hand out is harvestable and replayable**, which is why binding #78's nonce to the WC session would have looked like a fix and not been one. Fixed by stripping the reflected CORS headers for `gtm4wp/v2` only. | `src/Modules/VisitorData/VisitorDataEndpoint.php` |

**D-axis severity levelling (2026-08-04, maintainer-approved).** #76, #77 and #81 were
rated by three different passes and came out inconsistent with the threat model's own
rule — *rate on what the execution reaches*. Corrected in one pass so the ordering is
defensible:

- **#77 Low → Medium.** It shares #81's mechanism exactly (worktree-supplied code
  executing as the maintainer) and therefore its reach: shell, `gh` and SSH
  credentials, signing keys, push access to every repo the maintainer can push to.
  Only the *trigger* differs (a commit versus a build), and a trigger does not change
  reach. Low was a mis-rating, not a judgement call.
- **#76 stays Medium, but it is the least severe of the three**, not the most: an
  authenticated GitHub write is strictly narrower than arbitrary local execution. The
  original ordering had it above both.
- **Neither #77 nor #81 is rated High**, and the reason is worth stating because it is
  the thing that could change: the sink needs the maintainer to have checked out
  untrusted code, which is a deliberate act. If reviewing contributor branches locally
  ever becomes routine, both become High — the reach is already there.

**System changes made the same day** (this is the durable half): PA-7 rewritten + promoted ⭐ with a *grep the wrappers* litmus and a re-derivable call-site ledger; new **RI-17** (an escape is only valid at the instant of output), **RI-18** (sanitized ≠ authentic), **PA-14** (the toolchain is attack surface); **D0/D1** development-time actors added to the threat model with the toolchain brought in scope; **FP re-derivation rule** added to the suppressions preamble and FP-5 condition 3 tightened to "a CSRF gate bound to the caller"; two Coverage Matrix rows and the **Toolchain trust** sweep added here; `/code-review` pre-review step 1 widened to inventory the toolchain, plus new steps for FP re-derivation and post-fix family enumeration.
### 1.22.5 release testing: 2026-07-30 (no `/code-review` run)

Found by hand while smoke-testing the 1.22.5 backport on a dev store, not by a review pass — the tell was two dataLayer events from the same plugin disagreeing about a value's *type*. Recorded here because it affects **both** lines and neither has it logged.

| # | Sev | Status | Summary | File(s) |
|---|-----|--------|---------|---------|
| 79 | Low | fixed | Correctness (RI-16 family, sibling asymmetry): in the `remove_from_cart` handler the mini-cart branch resolves quantity with `parseInt( textContent )` while the cart-page branch takes `input.value` raw, which is always a string. Two consequences: (a) `remove_from_cart` reports `quantity: "1"` on the cart page and `quantity: 1` on the mini-cart, for the same product; (b) the `if ( 0 === qty ) return true;` guard uses strict equality, so a zero-quantity line on the cart page (`"0"`) does not match and an event fires with `quantity: "0"`, while the mini-cart correctly suppresses it — the two surfaces disagree on whether a zero-quantity removal is an event at all. `value` is unaffected (`price * "1"` coerces). Present verbatim in both lines. Fix: `parseInt` + `Number.isNaN` on both branches, per RI-16. | 2.0: `js/frontend/gtm4wp-woocommerce.js:569,576` · 1.x: `js/gtm4wp-woocommerce.js:362,369` |

| 80 | Medium | wontfix (1.x) | Functional gap, non-security: 1.x hooks only `woocommerce_blocks_product_grid_item_html`, which belongs to the **legacy** WooCommerce Blocks grid (`<li class="wc-block-grid__product">`). The modern **Product Collection** block (`<li class="wc-block-product post-{ID}">`, `wp-block-woocommerce-product-template`) fires neither that filter nor `woocommerce_after_shop_loop_item`, so on a current store using the default product block 1.x emits **no** product-data markup and therefore no `view_item_list` / `select_item` at all. Confirmed live on a dev store: zero `data-gtm4wp_product_data` markers on the page while `dataLayer_content` rendered normally. 2.0 already covers this via `ListTracking::add_productdata_to_product_collection_block()` on `render_block`. **Maintainer decision (2026-07-30): not backported.** 1.x is superseded by 2.0 in ~1 month; adding a feature to an EOL line creates a defect surface that would compete for 2.0's remaining runway. | 1.x: `integration/woocommerce.php:1527` (only block hook registered) |

No new pattern needed for #79 — **RI-16** already owns that class, and its litmus ("when three branches do the same normalization and one spells it differently, read the odd one") is exactly what it is. #69 was the same file's `add_to_cart` sibling; this is the third occurrence, worth noting when RI-16 is next reviewed.

**#75 upgraded from reasoned to demonstrated (2026-07-30).** While verifying the 1.22.5 X-Forwarded-For fix against a live dev store with the custom-header option enabled, the value was dictated from an unrelated machine: a single arbitrary address was returned verbatim as `visitorIP`, and sending two entries let the caller choose which one was reported. No authentication, no preconditions beyond the option being on. The finding stays **Low** — it is analytics integrity, and nothing in this repository makes an access decision on the value — but its exploitability is now confirmed trivial rather than inferred, which is what should drive its priority in 2.0.

**#75 fix session (2026-08-04) — and the half the analysis below missed.** The recorded
plan had two halves (trusted-proxy setting + admin signal) and both shipped. Scoping the
work surfaced a **third**, which no prior note contains: forwarding headers come in two
families, and only one of them is a list-ordering problem.

- **Append** (`X-Forwarded-For`) — the finding as written. Fixed by walking from the
  right and stopping at the first entry outside the trusted set.
- **Replace** (`CF-Connecting-IP`, `True-Client-IP`, `X-Real-IP` — all three already had
  passing tests) — the proxy *overwrites* the header, so its value is authoritative
  **only if the request actually came through that proxy**. Nothing checked that, so a
  request delivered straight to the origin with a forged `CF-Connecting-IP` was trusted
  verbatim. "Parse from the right" does nothing for this family, and the single-value
  branch would have been left looking correct.

One mechanism covers both, which is why the setting is a **trusted-address allow-list**
rather than the hop count the earlier note implied: knowing which upstreams you trust
answers "where does the client sit in this list?" *and* "did this header come from my
proxy at all?". Worth generalizing: **when a finding names one branch of a two-branch
function, check whether the other branch has the same defect in a different shape** —
here the sibling was not a variant of the bug, it was the bug with the list removed.

Also found while fixing, and logged as **#89**: the header-name allow-list was
unanchored on the **save** side — #62's defect at the entry point #62 did not cover.

**What the 2.0 fix needed — it was not just "parse from the right".** Two halves:

1. **A trusted-proxy setting.** Reading the list from the right is only correct once you know how many hops the site's own infrastructure appends; with no such configuration there is no authoritative element in the list at all. That is a settings addition, which is precisely why it did not belong in a 1.x patch.
2. **An admin-facing signal.** The option currently moves the admin from one wrong answer to a worse one, silently. With no custom header the value is the reverse proxy's address — identical for every visitor, observed on that same store where an unrelated client and the site owner both resolved to one constant. With `X-Forwarded-For` set it becomes attacker-chosen. Neither state is communicated anywhere. The field description should name the trade-off, and the `Options::hardcoded_errors()` → `Notices` pattern from #60 is the precedent for surfacing it in the admin rather than leaving it to be discovered.

**Fix session (2026-07-30).** #66–#73 and #79 **fixed**; #72 verified first (probe-confirmed, Conf. 0.75 → 1.0) and unified on the hex-flag encoder by maintainer decision, breaking the 1.x single-quoted output shape for the string branch — defensible because the array branch beside it had already broken that parity, so integrators had no consistent shape to rely on. #68 took true recency eviction rather than the cheaper comment correction, also by maintainer decision. Baseline after: **PHP 594 tests / 2076 assertions**, **JS 302 tests / 23 suites**, `phpcs` **0 errors** repo-wide, `npm run build` + `lint:js` clean.

Two scope calls worth recording. **#69/#79 were widened into a four-site family fix**: enumerating every DOM-sourced quantity found four sites, all spelled differently, with three different zero-behaviours, so fixing only the two logged ones would have left a fresh asymmetry of exactly the kind RI-16 describes. All four now route through one `gtm4wp_read_quantity()` helper, each site's existing zero/missing semantics preserved. The user-visible consequence — variation and grouped-product `add_to_cart` now report a numeric quantity where they sent a string — is in the changelog with the same "check your GTM setup" warning the `JSON_NUMERIC_CHECK` removal carries. **#68 uses a sequence counter, not a timestamp**: several products are stored in the same millisecond on a list page, and tied stamps fall back to `Object.keys` order — the very numeric ordering the fix exists to stop relying on.

**Six existing tests asserted the defects.** Three pinned the string `quantity`, three pinned the single-quoted `esc_js` shape. Each was read before being changed (BE-1), and the reasons are recorded in the test comments. More telling: the WooCommerce suite carried a comment describing double-pushed events as harness housekeeping and had loosened its assertions to tolerate them — a written description of #71's production symptom, read as a quirk. That is now **TS-14** in the test system, with the litmus that a comment explaining why an assertion is weak is a review lead.

**Method note.** #80 was found by accident: a test plan said "test the block product grid" without specifying *which* block, and the tester reasonably used the current default. The two block families take entirely different code paths, and only one of them is hooked on 1.x. Any test step touching WooCommerce product lists should name the block explicitly — legacy grid vs Product Collection vs classic shortcode loop — because "it showed nothing" means three different things across them. The same ambiguity would hide a real regression just as easily as it exposed this gap.

  bundle state now surviving a reload is what the fix is *for*, so tests simulating a
  **new page view** (a new window) must clear the flag explicitly, while a test
  simulating a **re-injection on the same page** must not — the distinction is now
  stated in a comment at each site. jsdom keeps one window per file, which is what
  makes it easy to model the wrong one silently.

**#81 — what was done, and the limit of it.** Three changes, maintainer-approved:
`Bash(npm run *)` and `Bash(npx wp-scripts *)` pinned to the scripts and subcommands
actually used (removes the arbitrary-script-name leg), and **`Bash(git checkout *)`
removed** so an agent session cannot switch to a contributor branch unattended.

**This narrows the finding; it does not close it, and the distinction matters enough
to record.** The permission gate governs what an *agent* runs, not what the maintainer
types — so the ordinary sequence (maintainer checks out a branch by hand, a session
then runs the pre-approved `npm run build`) is untouched, and the body of that script
is still whatever the branch's `package.json` defines. Pinning a command name can
never fix a dispatcher: only the *name* is in the allowlist, the *body* is in the
worktree. Closing it needs isolation — building contributor branches in a container,
which also closes #77 — or a standing habit of not opening agent sessions on untrusted
checkouts. Left **open** deliberately rather than marked fixed.

### Report 12: `.security/code-review-report-2026-08-04-1633.md`

**Reviewed at:** `58d2f8f` · **Base:** `b58d427` (range `b58d427..58d2f8f`, 4 commits) · **Fixes:** all 8 applied in the working tree — **record the fix commit sha here when it lands; it is the base for the next review.**

R11 left `Fixes:` unfilled again, so the base stayed R11's recorded `Reviewed at:` and
**R11's three fix sessions fell inside this range** — the #78 Origin gate, the #90 CORS
withdrawal, the #75 trusted-proxy allow-list, the `HardcodedContainers` extraction, and
the two commits that landed after the review closed. **No Critical/High/Medium. 8 Low
(#91–#98).**

The security work in range is sound where it touches, and the yield is a step past R11's
theme. R11 said *a fix's own litmus can be written too narrowly to reach its siblings*.
Three of these eight say something sharper: **a fix can be correct and still hand its
own successor a defect.** #91 is the new Origin gate undermined by the helper it chose
to read Referer with; #92 is a new call site added to a sink that was already broken;
#94 is one fix session solving "share the predicate, don't copy it" correctly for one
allow-list and by copy-paste for the other, three files apart, in the same commit.

Reached only when the client sends no `Origin` (current browsers always do on a cross-origin POST), which is what keeps it Low — but where reached, the effect is #78's effect. Fixed by reading the header directly; the docblock now says why that helper is the wrong one here. Test pins the mechanism (`->never()`), not just the outcome. | `src/Modules/WooCommerce/PageDataLayer.php` |
| 92 | Low | fixed | Correctness (#29's class, RI-3), **probe-verified**: the noscript sink echoed `wp_kses()` output with no ampersand restore while emitting `<script>` bodies containing `console.warn && console.warn(…)`, so the container-suppressed console warnings shipped as `&amp;&amp;` — a `SyntaxError`, no warning shown, a JS error instead. Live for placement OFF and the kill switch (both also in released 1.x) and inherited by the excluded-user-role branch added in this range. Fixed with a script-scoped restore (`ScriptTag::print_markup_block()`) so the iframe attribute keeps its correct `&amp;` form — a blanket restore would have fixed the script by corrupting the URL. | `src/Frontend/ContainerCode.php`, `src/Frontend/ScriptTag.php` |

**Fix session (2026-08-04):** all 8 fixed. Three calls worth recording:

- **#92 was probe-verified before being fixed and the probe is the test.** Reverting the
  sink for one run reproduced `console.warn &amp;&amp; console.warn(…)` exactly, which
  moved it from 0.90 to certain. The reason the suite was green is worth more than the
  bug: `test_the_tag_wraps_get_tag_through_kses_with_iframe_rules` stubs `wp_kses` as
  **identity**, twelve cases after a sibling that models the real one
  (`str_replace('&','&amp;')`) and comments "the real one does". **Two harnesses for one
  core function in one file, and the weaker one guarded the broken sink** — a TS-1 case
  that no coverage metric can show, because the line *is* covered.
- **The fix deliberately did NOT reuse `print_script_block()`.** Routing the whole block
  through it would have restored the ampersand inside the noscript iframe `src` too,
  turning a correct `&amp;`-joined URL into a raw `&` — fixing the script by degrading
  the attribute, and breaking parity with 1.x. The two contexts want opposite things
  from the same encoding, so the new sink restores **only inside `<script>` elements**,
  via `preg_replace_callback` (PA-7: a callback returns the replacement, so `$1` in a
  script body is never expanded) with a `null`-guard that degrades to the sanitized
  input rather than blanking the output (#85's rule).
- **#93's test asserts the contract, not the wording.** A data provider maps each option
  to the variable name and value-form terms its description must contain, including the
  two already-correct ones, so the group is pinned as a set — which is what RI-15's
  "descriptions drift as a set" actually asks for.

Verified after the fixes: **PHP 688 tests / 2279 assertions green** (+29 tests from the
review baseline of 659/2227), `phpcs` **0 errors** repo-wide (blessed warnings only).
**No `js/` source changed**, so no `build/` regeneration was required — confirmed with
`git status --porcelain -- js/ build/`. CHANGELOG + readme.txt updated together: one new
`* Fixed:` for #92 (a defect that shipped in released 1.x), and the #91/#93/#95/#96
changes folded into the existing unreleased bullets they belong to, per the
unreleased-feature rule. #94, #97 and #98 are internal with no user-visible change.

### External scan: 2026-08-04 (no `/code-review` run)

**Scanned at:** `c82f403` (Report 12 closed out) · **Source:** an independent multi-agent scan of the whole repository, run outside this system — 9 components, 29 category-lens passes plus a breadth sweep, 17 raw candidates deduplicated to 14, each put to a three-voter adversarial panel; **3 survived**. Detail lives in the git-ignored scan report directory, not here.

**One of the three is #77, re-found independently.** The scan's top finding is the worktree-resolved commit hook, verbatim — same file, same mechanism, and its panel confirmed it unanimously (3/3), where the two new findings passed 2/3. Two separate scanners five weeks apart now rate it the same way, which is corroboration for the **D-axis levelling** above rather than a new finding, so it keeps its number. Worth noting *how* it was re-found: the scan's own inventory set `.githooks/` and `.claude/` aside as "not executable product code reachable by an attacker" — the same reasoning that kept them absent from this checklist until 2026-07-29 — and its **breadth sweep found the hook anyway**. A skip ledger with a stated reason is not a gap, but the reason is a claim, and this one was wrong for the maintainer's machine while being right for the plugin's runtime.

**The third is recorded by number only** (#100). Its substance is in the local scan report and is deliberately not characterized here — not the class, not the area, not the severity — because a fix for it cannot land in this repository, which makes any committed row a pointer with nothing on the other side of the ledger. See the note below the table for the rule that produced that call.

As in 2026-07-29, the value of this entry is **why this system could not have produced #99**. It is not a coverage gap — it sits in a component the matrix marks `[x]`, reviewed the same day:

- **A weakener, not a sink (#99).** Every escaping pattern here asks whether *the plugin's own output* is safe: RI-2, RI-3, RI-4, PA-3 and PA-4 all follow a value outward to a sink. #99 is not a sink and carries no plugin data — it adjusts a WordPress control that governs *other code's* output for the rest of the request. Nothing in this system points at a hook that changes the rules for everyone else, so the lens that would have caught it did not exist. Sharper still: **R12 worked three findings deep in exactly this area** — #92 is the same `<noscript>` iframe's escaping — and never asked why that iframe needs the accommodation in the first place. The question "what does this plugin relax for its host?" is the missing one.

| # | Sev | Status | Summary | File(s) |
|---|-----|--------|---------|---------|
| 99 | Low (security, **A2**) | **fixed in 2.0** 2026-08-04 · **1.x: wontfix** (maintainer decision — security-only backports, and a Low hardening finding does not clear that bar on a line superseded within weeks) | Sanitizer scope: a hardening control that WordPress applies to content from users without `unfiltered_html` was relaxed more widely than the plugin's own markup requires. CSS-only, **no script execution**; the effect is content an editorial reviewer does not see rendered. Fixed by scoping the relaxation to the plugin's own sanitizer call and withdrawing it immediately after (`try`/`finally`), with the container output left byte-identical. Three regression tests pin it in both directions — widened *at* the sink, absent *after* it, and never registered at hook time. **Released 1.x keeps the code**, so the actor, the trigger and the reachable effect stay in the local scan report; the changelog describes the behavior change without the security framing, for the same reason. | `src/Frontend/ContainerCode.php` · 1.x: `public/frontend.php` (wontfix) |
| 100 | — | **not recorded here** 2026-08-04 | Number reserved so it is never re-used (see the numbering note at the top of this log). Substance in the local scan report, per the withholding rule below. | — |

**Why #99 is Low despite an A2 actor.** The threat model rates by the lowest actor who can reach the sink, and that is A2 (contributor/author) rather than the A4 who owns the setting — but the reachable effect is presentational, not executable, and the multisite `unfiltered_html` caveat is the ceiling rather than the floor here. It is defense-in-depth that the plugin removed for the whole request when it only needed it for one line of its own output. Rated Low deliberately, not by default.

**The withholding rule, which is the durable half of this entry.** Every finding in this log up to now points at code that a commit can change, so writing the row down is a step toward fixing it and the disclosure cost buys something. That is not universal. **Before writing a finding into this file, ask whether a fix can land in this repository at all.** Where the answer is no — where the only remediation is an action taken somewhere else entirely — a committed row cannot *be* progress toward the fix; it can only point, on a public repo, at something no later commit can retract. The rule that follows: reserve the number so it is never re-used, keep the substance in the git-ignored report, act on it outside git, and carry the lesson into a pattern **phrased so that it names no location and reads the same in any repository**. A pattern that would let a reader work out where to look has not been generalized, only relocated.

**System changes made the same day** (the rest of the durable half): new **PA-15** (never relax a host security control globally to accommodate your own output — scope it to the call it exists for), written as a class description with the instance detail withheld, exactly as PA-7 was handled for #74; **PA-14 extended with a fifth shape**, the repository's own history; and the matching standing note on the **Toolchain trust** sweep. The gap it names stands on its own regardless of what prompted it: every sweep in this system reads the working tree, none has ever read the history, and that makes it the one shape a diff-scoped review is structurally guaranteed never to reach. Stated plainly rather than attributed to something else — a record that shades the truth to help a concealment is worth less than the concealment gains, and this class is cheap to look for anyway (a secret scanner over all refs is a universal first move, available to anyone without reading this file). What a public file must not hand over is the *location*; the class costs little.

### Report 13: `.security/code-review-report-2026-08-05-0752.md`

R11's theme was *a fix's litmus too narrow to reach its siblings*; R12's was *a fix that
hands its own successor a defect*. This range adds a third: **the toolchain grew a new

Probe-verified. Fixed by removing the hook (CI runs the same check and the hook's own comment already called CI the real gate). **Residual, deliberately recorded:** removal closes the automatic trigger only — a manual standards run and the CI step both still read the branch's config, which is the same residual #77 and #81 carry, and the local one is not contained. | `.githooks/pre-commit` (removed), `phpcs.xml` |

Payload is route metadata only (no user data, no tokens), so the reachable effect is plugin-presence fingerprinting; the finding is that the class docblock, this checklist's row and the test provider all asserted namespace-wide coverage. The countervailing duty-to-warn is real, so the fix keeps the admin-facing warning and drops the mechanism detail and the label. Both files edited together. | `CHANGELOG.md`, `readme.txt` |

**Toolchain posture after R13 — what #101 revealed, and what is actually closed.**
Answering "how do we close #101?" turned up the more useful fact: **phpcs was one of
eight equivalent sinks**, and the least dangerous of them. Enumerated —
`vendor/bin/phpunit` (executes `tests/bootstrap.php` **and every test file**, by
design), `composer install` (`allow-plugins`, lifecycle scripts), `npm ci` (install
hooks), `npm run build` (executes `webpack.config.js`), `npm run test:unit` (jest config
+ every JS test file), `npm run lint:js` (executes `.eslintrc.js`), `.githooks/commit-msg`
(#77), `vendor/bin/phpcs` (#101). The project's own documented workflow requires most of
them, and PHPUnit is strictly worse than the one that was reported.

Two consequences worth keeping:

- **Per-tool pinning is whack-a-mole.** Pinning phpcs's ruleset outside the worktree
  would have closed one of eight and not the important one. That is why #101 was fixed
  by removing the trigger rather than by hardening the tool.
- **Only isolation is an *enforced* boundary** (threat model: "note whether the boundary
  is enforced or merely described. Only the enforced one counts"). Running the tooling in
  a container closes #77, #81 and #101 together. *Not checking out contributor branches*
  reduces the trigger to zero but is a **described** boundary — mitigation, not a fix.
  CODEOWNERS does not help at all: it gates merging, and the checkout happens first.

**`composer check-branch` / `npm run check-branch` (`tools/check-branch.js`) was added
as the cheap complement**, and its own header says plainly what it is not. It diffs the
executable wiring against the merge base so an honest branch's build/test changes get
read before anything runs. It is **not a control**: a hostile branch supplies the script
too, and a clean report still leaves every file under `tests/` executable by PHPUnit —
which the output states rather than leaving implied. Record it as a prompt; do not let a
future review count it as the fix for this class.

**Fix session (2026-08-05):** all 7 fixed. Four calls worth recording:

- **#101 was fixed by deletion, and the residual is in the row above on purpose.** The
  tempting fix — pin the config path outside the worktree — leaves the same class
  reachable from the manual run the project's own `CLAUDE.md` recommends. Removing the
  hook is honest about what it buys: the automatic, unattended trigger. It does not
  close the class, and #77/#81 are still open next to it.
- **#105's fix encodes the WHOLE message, not just the value.** Encoding only the value
  would have forced string concatenation into the emitted JS and broken both 1.x output
  parity and the two existing byte assertions for no gain. Encoding the whole literal
  leaves the line byte-identical for an ordinary role slug — which is what made the
  existing tests keep passing while the new hostile-input test still fails without the
  fix.
- **#106's test had to model two sanitizers faithfully to say anything at all.** The
  shared fixture stubs the text sanitizer as identity (correctly, for every other test
  in the file), and under an identity stub the two functions are indistinguishable while
  the line still shows as covered. The new test stubs the one behaviour that separates
  them. This is the #92 lesson — an identity stub for a core function hides the class it
  is standing in for — arriving a second time, in a different file.
- **The new blocking-warnings setting caught its first real thing immediately:** the
  added data-provider rows tripped WPCS alignment warnings, which now fail the build.
  `phpcbf` fixed 13 of them and one docblock error was fixed by hand.

Verified after the fixes: **PHP 696 tests / 2294 assertions green** (+5 tests, +8
assertions from the review baseline of 691/2286), `phpcs` **exit 0** repo-wide — and
that is now a stronger statement than in any previous report, because `c92261f` made
warnings blocking, so the long-standing "blessed unused-mock-param warnings only"
caveat no longer applies to any entry after this one. **No `js/` source changed** in the
range or in the fix session, so no `build/` regeneration was required (confirmed with
`git status --porcelain -- js/ build/`). CHANGELOG + readme.txt updated by **editing**
two existing unreleased bullets, per the unreleased-feature rule.

**Verification (no findings):** **PA-15 swept for the first time** — 33 `add_filter()`
calls enumerated, exactly one loosens a host control and #99 already scoped it; the one
other widening filter targets a third-party minifier, not a security control. **FP-2
re-derived** (oldest-first): conclusion holds — the additional-pushes path still emits
through `wp_add_inline_script` with nothing decoding it, and the object push carries the
full hex flags. Worth adding to that entry: two of its three legs are deliberately
unescaped developer-supplied JS, which the FP as written does not say. All four
container patterns anchored and applied **at the sink**; the mixed-markup script sink
cannot emit a break-out character and degrades rather than blanking on a PCRE failure;
the guest-beacon Origin gate re-read end to end. Mechanical: ABSPATH on **all 57** PHP
files, **149** constants with zero dead, **62** hook callbacks all resolving, **19** JS
bundles none dead, **35** superglobal reads all unslashed and validated, **22**
`wp_json_encode` call sites with every script-context one hex-flagged, **0**
`$wpdb`/`unserialize()`/`eval()`/entity-decode, **0** order `get_post_meta()`. Surface
grep reconciles exactly. `npm audit --omit=dev`: 0 vulnerabilities, and the plugin
declares no production npm dependencies at all.

### Report 14: `.security/code-review-report-2026-08-05-1421.md`

**Reviewed at:** `4ce7537` · **Base:** `d38990b` (range `d38990b..4ce7537`, 4 commits, **one production commit**) · **Fixes:** all 6 applied in the working tree — **record the fix commit sha here when it lands; it is the base for the next review.**

R13 recorded its fix commit, so for the first time in five reviews the base was not a
review's own HEAD. R13's entry nonetheless handed its **own production code** forward to
be read deliberately rather than inherited, and R14 did that: `RestCors`, the #105
encoder swap, the #106 Referer sanitizer, and the new `tools/check-branch.js`.
**No Critical/High/Medium. 6 Low (#108–#113), all fixed.**

**The theme is that a ledger is a measurement, not a fact.** Three of the six are this
system's own bookkeeping asserting something the tree contradicts — and one of them was
written *the previous day*, by the review that created the rule it broke. R11's theme was
*a fix's litmus too narrow to reach its siblings*; this is a step further in: **a litmus
that reaches its siblings correctly, and then records its result as a standing all-clear.**
"Pile (b) is empty", "22 call sites", "do NOT flag" — each is the sentence that stops the
next reviewer running the grep, which is the same failure as a stale matrix date (#73) and
a missing row (R7) wearing a third disguise.

| # | Sev | Status | Summary | File(s) |
|---|-----|--------|---------|---------|
| 108 | Low | fixed | Correctness (new **RI-19**): the checkout `wp_footer` fallback added in range keyed on `! wp_add_inline_script()`, which is false both when the handle is already printed (the case it was written for) and when it was never registered — and the second is the ordinary state of a **block-based** cart/checkout, where the module deliberately loads the block tracker instead while `is_checkout()` stays true. So WooCommerce's default checkout got a standalone `<script>` carrying the whole checkout item array that nothing on the page reads, duplicating the items the `begin_checkout` push already carries. Not an exposure (same data, same page, visitor's own cart); the cost is a dead duplicate payload on the common path. Fixed with a positive precondition (`wp_script_is( …, 'enqueued' )`); a handle another plugin dequeued is covered by the same test. Regression case verified to fail without it. | `src/Modules/WooCommerce/PageDataLayer.php` |
| 109 | Low (D0→D1, no added reach) | fixed | Toolchain (PA-14): the watch-list in the script R13 added to *prompt* about #101 matched exact filenames, but a branch supplies the filename as surely as the contents — and PHP_CodeSniffer reads `.phpcs.xml` **before** `phpcs.xml`, so a branch could bring a higher-precedence ruleset while the report said "no executable wiring changed". Its failure mode is false reassurance, which is the only thing a prompt can get wrong. Fixed by enumerating every config name each tool accepts (dotfile and `.dist` variants, `.npmrc`, the eslint/babel/postcss/jest configs) instead of the one file in the tree, plus a stated caveat that git-ignored files inside a watched directory are outside the uncommitted-changes leg. | `tools/check-branch.js` |
| 110 | Low | fixed | Correctness + ledger (PA-4/RI-4): the `esc_js`'d nonce in the admin dismiss handler is a string **value** in a raw `<script>` body — pile (b) of the two-pile ledger written the previous day, which recorded pile (b) as **empty**. Inert (a nonce is 10 ASCII characters, so `esc_js` is a provable no-op) and A4-only, so the code half is the #72/#105 consistency class. The ledger half is the finding: the hit had been filed under "constrained", a **third category the piles do not have**, and "the value happens to be harmless" is a statement about today's data rather than about the encoder. Fixed by encoding the whole literal (byte-identical for a real nonce); the ledger now says to sort by what the value *is in the emitted JS*. Hostile-input regression test, both directions, probe-verified to discriminate between the two encoders. | `src/Admin/Notices.php`, `.security/code-review-patterns.md` |
| 111 | Low | fixed | Ledger accuracy (#87's class, one review later, in the same sweep row): the Duplication sweep recorded 22 `wp_json_encode` call sites where there are **23**, and 7 attribute-context sites where there are **8** — the visitor-cart fragment was missing — while "2 blessed flagless" mixed an encoder call with a helper call. No escaping defect: every script-context site carries the full hex flags. Fixed by re-deriving the breakdown **with its counting rule stated**, so the next re-derivation produces a comparable figure instead of a differently-wrong one. The Dead-JS row had drifted the same way (three different importer counts for an unchanged tree in three runs) and now carries its rule too. | `.security/code-review-checklist.md` |
| 112 | Low (security, A4 — hardening, no live break-out) | fixed | Escape-then-transform (**RI-17**): an `oembed_result` callback spliced a site-derived origin into the iframe `src` **after** the oEmbed handler had escaped that markup, with no escaping of its own. A4-set and structurally constrained (a hostname cannot carry a quote), so nothing was exploitable — the point is that an escape which is only correct because of where its value happens to come from is not an escape. Fixed with `esc_url()` at the splice plus null-gated `wp_parse_url` keys (an unresolvable site URL now leaves the embed untouched instead of raising a warning on every embed). The generalizable half is new to RI-17: **every mechanism it listed starts from *our* escaper**, so its litmus selects nothing in a filter callback that edits markup somebody else escaped. Regression test models the real `esc_url` allow-list — Brain Monkey's stub does not strip, so a pass-through would have made the assertion vacuous; verified to fail without the fix, with the attribute break-out visible in the failure output. | `src/Modules/MediaEvents/MediaEventsModule.php` |
| 113 | Low | fixed | Stale suppression, re-derived under the oldest-first rule (FP-3): closed by construction — zero `$echo` parameters remain and `phpcs` exits 0 with warnings blocking, so the waived warning cannot be raised. **The correction that mattered was to the packaging, not the entry**: the body had said "resolved" since R4 while the **Quick Index** row still read live in the present tense under "do NOT flag", and coverage notes still quoted it as a current caveat. The stale claim lived in the summary people read rather than in the entry they do not. Rule added: when you resolve an FP, fix its index row and grep the checklist for prose that still quotes it. **Next review re-derives FP-4.** | `.security/code-review-patterns.md` |

**Fix session (2026-08-05).** Four calls worth recording:

- **#108's fix is a positive precondition, not a longer negative.** Adding "…and it was
  registered" to the falsy test would have read as a special case; asking `wp_script_is(
  …, 'enqueued' )` states the actual requirement — *there is a reader on this page* — and
  covers the dequeued-by-an-optimizer case the finding never mentioned. `'enqueued'`
  rather than `'registered'` for the same reason, and it stays true after the script
  prints, so the already-done branch is still reached.
- **The `4ce7537` test whose name said "unregistered" had its premise inverted, not its
  assertions.** It set the attach to fail and asserted the fallback fired; under the fix
  that is only correct if the handle is present, so it was renamed to what it now models
  (*the attach fails*) and a new case covers the absent handle. A test that keeps passing
  while the behaviour it describes has changed is worse than one that goes red.
- **The identity `wp_kses` stub added in range was replaced with a realistic one.** R14's
  report raised it as a `/test-review` item rather than a finding (tests are out of
  findings scope); it was fixed here anyway, because the new assertions are about a
  `print_script_block()` sink and an identity stub makes exactly those vacuous — #92's
  lesson, in a second file, in the commit under review.
- **#112's test had to model the real `esc_url` to say anything.** Brain Monkey's stub
  rewrites `&` and `'` and strips nothing, so the hostile host survives it intact; the
  test stubs the character allow-list that *is* the escaping. Same shape as #106's
  sanitizer pair and #92's `wp_kses` — the third time in three reviews that a faithful
  stub was the difference between a test and a covered line.

Verified after the fixes: **PHP 706 tests / 2325 assertions green** (+8 tests, +17 assertions from the review baseline of 698/2308), `phpcs` **exit 0**
repo-wide with warnings blocking. **No `js/` source changed** in the range or the fix
session, so no `build/` regeneration was required (`git status --porcelain -- js/ build/`
empty).

**CHANGELOG: one bullet, and getting there was its own instance of the review's theme.**
The fix session first concluded "no entry" for all three code fixes, on the reasoning
that each is either 2.0-only code from this same unreleased cycle (#108, #110) or a no-op
for real input (#112 — `esc_url()` returns an ordinary origin unchanged). The Stop hook
blocked, and re-deriving the claim against the branch — which the `changelog` skill
explicitly tells you to do (`git grep <symbol> 1.x`) — showed the third one was wrong:
`integration/youtube.php:24-27` on `origin/1.x` carries the **identical** unguarded
`wp_parse_url()` read, so the null-gating half of #112 *is* a delta against 1.22.5. A site
whose WordPress Address is not a parseable URL gets a PHP warning on every YouTube embed
and a broken `origin=` on released 1.x, and does not on 2.0.

Two things worth keeping. **(1)** The `esc_url()` half is genuinely no-op-for-real-input,
and that correct observation is what carried the incorrect conclusion for the *other*
half of the same fix — a single fix can be a delta on one axis and not on another, so
decide per behaviour, not per commit. **(2)** This is the review's own theme landing on
the review: *parity with 1.x was asserted rather than measured*, which is what #110, #111
and #113 are all about. Recorded as a **`* Fixed:`** bullet, and specifically as an
**extension of the existing "malformed YouTube embed URL" bullet** rather than a new one —
same function, same user-visible symptom, one thing for the upgrading user to read.
Described as behaviour only, with no security framing and no mechanism: released 1.x still
carries the unescaped splice, so the #99 precedent applies. Mirrored into `readme.txt`.

**Verification highlights (no findings):** the production change's new script sink is
sound — full hex flags at `:537`, and the `wp_kses` + ampersand-restore path is a
**provable no-op** on that payload because `JSON_HEX_AMP` leaves no literal `&` to encode
or restore; the `wp_kses` allow-list carries exactly the five attributes `opening_tag()`
emits, so the block survives intact **including the CSP nonce**, which the previous
`wp_add_inline_script` path did not. Ordering verified rather than assumed: the tracker's
reads are lazy (`DOMContentLoaded`/per-event), which is why all three historical
placements work — 1.x printed at `wp_footer:25` (after the tracker), the normal 2.0 path
prints `'before'` it, and the fallback prints at `wp_footer:5`. **The whole
`wp_add_inline_script` family was checked for the same exposure and is clean** — the only
other site running after `wp_print_head_scripts` is `DataLayer::flush_pushes()`, and it
survives solely because its handle is hard-wired `$in_footer = true` while
`gtm4wp-woocommerce`'s placement is filterable. Mechanical: ABSPATH on all **57** PHP
files, **149** constants with zero dead, **63** hook callbacks all resolving, **19** JS
bundles none dead, **31** superglobal reads all unslashed and validated, **23**
`wp_json_encode` call sites with every script-context one hex-flagged, **17**
`print_script_block`/`print_markup_block` callers with every data-bearing one hex-encoding
upstream (FP-1's actual precondition), **0** `$wpdb`/`unserialize()`/`eval()`/entity-decode,
**0** order `get_post_meta()`. Surface grep reconciles exactly; **#107 confirmed landed**
in `d3a3816` by checking both unreleased blocks rather than the commit message.
Deep-pass re-audit of the oldest `[x]` rows (MediaEvents 07-13, ContactForm7 07-14,
ConsentMode 07-14/15, Module Framework 07-10) produced #112 and nothing else; the
ConsentMode bridges interpolate only the validated data-layer identifier.

| 182 | **Medium** (correctness — fatal; upstream hook contract, generalized as **RI-25**) | **fixed 2026-08-12** | Re-applying an upstream filter carried its name but not its **argument list**. `WP_Hook` never pads a short argument list, so a site callback written to the vendor's documented signature raised an uncaught `ArgumentCountError` on the order-received page — fatal, not degraded. Introduced by the commit under review. No site actor: the trigger is the site's own callback, though the page it kills is visitor-facing. **The suite structurally could not see it** — a mocked `apply_filters` with an optional-param stand-in passes either way (UC-3); the regression test declares its parameters **required** and was watched red, its trace landing on the finding's own line. **The recommendation was measured across all three callback shapes before being written.** Family swept: **11** third-party hook sites, 10 correct, 1 wrong — the newest. | `src/Modules/WooCommerce/PageDataLayer.php`, `tests/unit/Modules/PageDataLayerTest.php` |
| 183 | Low (correctness — a branch whose domain is wider than its stated justification; RI-22/RI-23 family) | **fixed 2026-08-12** | A feature-guard fallback is justified in its docblock by one upstream version window while the branch actually governs a wider one, so on the older part of the supported range it withholds data where upstream makes no such decision. Bounded and fail-closed. R23's "no safe version test exists — documentation fix only" conclusion was **superseded, not contradicted**: no version *number* test is safe, but the behaviour and a probe-able symbol (`WC_Shortcode_Checkout::guest_should_verify_email`, `private static`, seen by `method_exists`) shipped in the **same** upstream release, so probing the symbol scopes the whole mirror with no version constant — gates absent means upstream renders to any valid key holder, so nothing is withheld. | 188 | Low (exposure — the fallback's guest-only shape vs upstream's wider domain; found and fixed inside #183's fix session, same day) | **fixed 2026-08-12** | The helper-absent fallback published for every customer order reaching it, on the stated premise that the known-shopper gate above had already covered them. Measured at the 8.4.0/8.5.2 tags, the premise is wrong for one state: with `woocommerce_order_received_verify_known_shoppers` filtered **off**, upstream still routes a non-owner into the guest email verification — its only customer-id term is the logged-in-owner short-circuit — so past the grace period upstream hides while the fallback published the identity blocks. Leak direction, unlike #183's withhold direction, and #173's Medium is the precedent — rated Low only because it additionally requires the site to carry a non-default filter opt-out. Fixed by the same restructure: the fallback now mirrors the guest gate term by term for **every** order shape reaching it, with the logged-in-owner short-circuit keeping the buyer exempt (constraint 1 pinned by a regression test). A test asserting the old premise ("the fallback models the GUEST decision only") was rewritten — it had encoded an unmeasured claim. | `src/Modules/WooCommerce/PageDataLayer.php`, `tests/unit/Modules/PageDataLayerTest.php` |

> filter state — **zero unexplained divergences**; the five intended behaviour changes were

> M4 final-say filter, M5 owner short-circuit). R24 should re-adjudicate this change with

> bucket), so no data grounds raising it — and the feature-detect makes the floor free, so
> the choice costs nothing. One test that had pinned an unmeasured premise was rewritten

>

> index; `git worktree list` alone yields a false negative (a `remove` that left the branch)
> and a false positive (a hand-deleted worktree needing `prune`); plain `remove` exits 128 on a
> dirty worktree, which is how leftovers arise. The `.gitignore` entry stays as an annotated

**1 High + 1 Medium + 10 Low (#161–#172).** The High is a **disclosure** finding, not a code
defect. The Medium is on the **D-axis** and sits in a permission scope no run of this system
has ever read.

> **#161 / 1.x disposition, decided 2026-08-12 by the maintainer.** **`1.x` will not be
> patched**, on measured grounds rather than an estimate: `1.x`'s purchase event carries
> **no** billing fields at all (verified — the only PII path there is `orderData`), and
> `GTM4WP_OPTION_INTEGRATE_WCORDERDATA` defaults to **`false`** (`1.x:common/readoptions.php:195`),
> so the affected set is *stores that deliberately enabled order data*, reachable only by
> someone already holding one specific order's random key. There is no enumeration path.
> Recorded as **wontfix (1.x)** rather than dropped.
>
> **That decision changes the disclosure question rather than closing it**, and this is the
> part worth reading: "detail withheld pending the other line's fix" was the framing, and with
> no fix coming, published text describing the behaviour becomes **permanent** rather than
> embargoed. All three committed surfaces were therefore redacted at HEAD — impact and area
> kept, mechanism, trigger, upstream version boundary and time window removed. **Residual,
> stated rather than implied:** git history and the pushed commit message still carry the full
> description and cannot be retracted without a force-push that would neither remove the object
> from GitHub (it stays reachable by sha unless Support purges it) nor reach existing clones,
> forks, watch notifications and archive mirrors — and would itself point at what it hid. The
> redaction is a reach-reduction measure with a known ceiling, and it is recorded as such.
>
> **#163 disposition, decided 2026-08-12 — and the measurement that decided it came AFTER
> the finding was drafted, which is the point.** The maintainer's constraint was that
> *occasionally delivering a duplicate conversion is the worst outcome*, which eliminates the
> "emit but do not record" variant outright — it is the only one of the three that manufactures
> duplicates. What remained was measured rather than argued: the eligibility gauntlet runs the
> already-recorded test **before** the emit and before the write, so once the buyer has loaded
> their own confirmation page — which is where checkout sends them immediately — a later visit
> by anyone else emits nothing and records nothing. The window therefore needs the buyer to
> **never** have seen that page *and* somebody else to arrive first. Inside that window the
> count stays correct (exactly one) and only the attribution is wrong. **Both remaining
> alternatives buy that attribution by trading one rare failure for another** — one loses a
> guest's conversion permanently on a second device, the other loses it for an
> admin-created order paid without logging in — and each adds a branch to carry the
> difference. Kept as-is: it is the only option with neither duplicates nor losses, and the
> genuinely harmful half was already fixed in range. **Do not re-raise without new frequency
> data**; if it is ever revisited, note that the two withhold reasons are *not*
> interchangeable, so a design taking the single flag as its input will be wrong on one of
> them. Note also `GTM4WP_OPTION_INTEGRATE_WCNOORDERTRACKEDFLAG`: with it on, both the
> already-recorded test and the write short-circuit, so that mode has no de-duplication by
> design and this analysis does not apply to it.
>
> ⛔ **#161 and #163 are recorded terse on purpose.** Both concern behaviour the released
> `1.x` line still carries, so per the disclosure rule at the top of this file their
> mechanism, trigger and affected feature stay in the git-ignored report. Do not "improve"
> these rows by adding detail — the whole point of #161 is that this already happened once.

The `_ga_tracked` half is deliberately **not** pinned: #163 is `wontfix`, so there is no decision to enforce; pin it if that is ever revisited. | `tests/unit/Modules/PageDataLayerTest.php` |
| 172 | Low (robustness) — drafted at Conf. 0.7 | **fixed 2026-08-12** (documented, not re-engineered) | The phone-table generator fetches its source on every run; the default invocation has no local-cache path, so regeneration is not offline-safe. **Fixed 2026-08-12 by documentation rather than re-engineering**, which is the proportionate answer: the generator already accepts a path, so the real gap was that neither the composer script description nor the usage block said so. Both now do, and the usage block adds what a bare "you can pass a file" would have missed - that the argument chooses the source this script turns into shipped PHP, so a file somebody sent you is a supply-chain decision rather than a convenience (PA-18). No cache added: a cache is a second copy of somebody else's data with no staleness signal. | `tools/generate-phone-table.php` |

`wontfix` and fixed** as a side effect of #153's. All ledgers re-derived **after** the
session's code was staged, per #155's own lesson: `wp_json_encode` **17**, script sinks
**12**, array callbacks **64**, constants **151**, `global $…` **8**, `js/`+`build/`
clean. Suite **1461 → 1946 tests / 4026 → 4529 assertions**, green in
`--order-by=random` runs; `phpcs` exits 0 repo-wide; PHP 8.0 syntax lint clean over
`src/ compat/ tools/` and the root files. Generator verified **idempotent** (a re-run
reproduces both files byte for byte) and verified to **refuse and write nothing** on a
truncated source. Also corrected: **this report's own #153 recommendation was wrong** and
is marked as such rather than replaced — it would have broken Italian mobiles. **U111
retired** in `.upstream/`: its coupling site was deleted on 2026-08-11 and the row
outlived it, which is the same sibling-left-behind shape one system over.

**The theme is the same one five reviews running, and this time it is the *fix* that
left the sibling behind rather than a call site.** #145 was rated Medium last review
for a default that asserted more than it could carry; its fix replaced that default
with a genuine three-category table — and then gave the new category an **early
return**, which quietly opted 101 territories out of every test below it. #156 is why
nobody noticed: the docblock describes the new branch as a seven-country curiosity.
Generalized as **RI-23** — *an early return added for a newly recognised case takes
every later branch with it, and the count of rows entering that branch is the thing to
measure before calling it a special case.*

**#154 is the counter-example and it is a genuinely new shape.** Every D-axis finding
so far has been about code reaching the **maintainer's** machine — a hook, a
permission, a config a tool loads. This one is about code reaching **every user's**:
the repository now contains a program that writes the program that ships. Generalized
as **PA-18**.

| 145 | Medium (correctness, silent-wrong; no site actor) | **fixed** | The E.164 phone normalizer is wrong well beyond the one country it was raised for. **Researched exhaustively after the review** by running the function against the reference implementation's own example numbers for every territory in WooCommerce's calling-code table — **486 cases, 26 failures across 15 territories**, from four territory-level causes: (a) countries with **no** trunk prefix whose leading `0` is part of the national number (7 confirmed, up to 12 possible) — the case the finding was raised for; (b) the plugin's own trunk table is **short one country and wrong for another**, so one of its five entries covers only half the forms its country uses; (c) **WooCommerce's table is a UI hint, not a country-code table** — four entries embed an area code (three of those overflow the 15-digit bound, so the number is **dropped entirely**) and two are out of date; (d) #151's class, confirmed as a class. **Plus two cross-cutting defects a per-territory sweep cannot see, and they are the largest by volume**: the `+NN (0) …` courtesy form standard across DE/AT/CH/NL, and the assumption that every international prefix is `00` (**72 of 245** territories use something else). Every case produces a well-formed, length-valid, wrong number — nothing is rejected and nothing is logged, because a hash either matches or does not. Detail, the measured library comparison and four costed options are in the local report's **Appendix A**; **Appendix B** costs the reference library under the **PHP 8.0 floor, which stays** (its last 8.0-compatible build measured **0 disagreements** against the current one across 477 cases / 241 territories, so the staleness objection does not survive measurement); **Appendix C is the accepted fix and the correction to both**. Appendix A costed "extend the code" as four hand-maintained exception tables, which is the wrong design; **one table *generated* from the same upstream metadata — country → (calling code, national prefix) — takes the territory sweep from 456/482 to 482/482 for 7.3 KB**, versus 1.9 MB for the library, and fixes Italy, the whole no-trunk-prefix class, TM, MH and every WooCommerce calling-code defect at once, the last simply by no longer reading that table. Known residue, recorded rather than hidden: Argentina/Brazil real-world mobile spellings, non-`00` international prefixes, and #151 need further generated columns — the trigger to switch to the library instead of growing a third and fourth column. Retires upstream row **U110** (replacing "5 hand-written trunk prefixes, yearly" with "generated from upstream metadata, regenerate on release") and narrows **U57**. **Fixed the same day**, exactly as costed: a generated `CountryPhoneData` table (245 countries) + a generator in `tools/`, with `NATIONAL_TRUNK_PREFIXES`, `DEFAULT_TRUNK_PREFIX` and the WooCommerce calling-code reach **deleted**, plus the courtesy-zero and extension handling the typing set called for. Pinned by **489 generated oracle cases** (both halves read out of the upstream metadata, so the fixture fails when the code disagrees with the numbering plan rather than when it changes) and 8 hand-written cases; **16 of the 21 new assertions probe-verified red against the pre-fix code**. Suite 962→**1457 tests / 4016 assertions**, green under `--order-by=random`; `phpcs` clean. #151 and the AR/BR/IDD classes stay open **by design**, named in the docblock and in U110's "deliberately NOT modelled" clause. | `src/Modules/WooCommerce/Helpers.php` |

The sibling identifier **three lines below** omits its key for exactly this case and cites RI-13 in a comment while doing it, and the method's own docblock promises that empty fields are omitted. Probe-verified. The fix that wrote the rule down did not reach the line above it. **Fixed 2026-08-11**: guarded like its sibling, with the guard kept at the CALL SITE rather than pushed into the helper - `''` is the honest answer to "hash this", and only the caller knows the key is optional. 2 regression tests (the key absent for a folded-away gmail tag; a tagged address at any other domain still hashed, so the guard cannot become "drop tagged emails"), **probe-verified red** against the pre-fix code. | `src/Modules/WooCommerce/ProductData.php` |
| 147 | Low (duplication / RI-21) | **fixed** | The **eleventh** expression-position JSON sink of #141's class, and the one #141's ledger did not reach: the literal is built in a separate statement and interpolated into three JS expression positions. **Not reachable** — the value is a `(string)`-cast scalar — so this is consistency rather than a live defect. What makes it a finding is that it is neither routed through the shared `ScriptTag::json_literal()` guard nor documented as exempt, while a sibling sink documents precisely this exemption with its reason. #141's own recorded lesson (the class survives inside a shape the grep does not select) landing on #141's own ledger. | `src/Modules/WooCommerce/PageDataLayer.php` |
| 148 | Low (D0→D1; **no added reach**) | **fixed** | A new PHPUnit config landed in range and the branch-wiring prompt script does not watch it. Its list enumerates the config names a tool **discovers**; this one is **passed explicitly** by a documented composer script, so a branch changing it gets "No executable wiring changed." A PHPUnit config names a bootstrap and can carry extensions, which is #101's shape. The script is a prompt by its own header, so the harm is **false reassurance** — #109's failure mode, in the same file, a second time. Fix shape: watch the *shape* (`phpunit*.xml`), not the filenames in the tree today, which is the list's own stated rule. **Fixed 2026-08-11** exactly that way and **verified mechanically** (`git ls-files -- 'phpunit*.xml'` now returns both configs), plus a header note recording the general half: a discovery-shaped list cannot see a config a documented command *passes*, so read the project's own script definitions and check every path that appears after a config flag. | `tools/check-branch.js` |

The move was the right call — a forwarder would have been the over-abstraction the sweep looks for — and the cost of doing it right is that every prose reference to the old name goes stale silently. Generalizable step: **grep the old name, not just the callers, whenever a method moves.** **Fixed 2026-08-11**: both repointed. | `src/Frontend/ScriptTag.php`, `src/Admin/Notices.php` |
| 150 | Low (coverage bookkeeping / new surface) | **fixed** (row added) | A new top-level directory landed with **no Coverage Matrix row** — #135's "absent, not `[ ]`" a second time, in a directory outside `src/` that none of the inventory greps select. It is not plugin code and never ships in the release ZIP, but it is an artifact the project **publishes for users to import into their own tag manager**, so it can carry executable content and the exporter's real account identifiers. Reviewed this run and **clean on both**. Row added carrying those two questions, since no other lens here asks either. | `.gtm-container-template/` |
| 151 | Low (correctness, deliberate limitation) | ~~wontfix~~ → **fixed 2026-08-12 (R20)** | **Reopened and closed by #153's fix, which is the part worth reading**: this was closed as "a deliberate limitation, because telling the two spellings apart needs per-country possible lengths — a third column, and the third column is where adopting the library wins". Both halves of that turned out to be wrong under measurement. Possible lengths would not have fixed it (Italy's two readings are both possible Italian lengths); the general national-number pattern does, at 32 bytes per territory; and the "third column" framing was the wrong axis for the decision, which actually turns on facts-versus-transform-rules. Kazakhstan's bare mobile spelling now resolves correctly, **probe-verified red** against the pre-fix code. **The general lesson, and it is about this ledger rather than the code: a `wontfix` carries a cost estimate, and a cost estimate is a measurement nobody took.** This one stood for a day and was off by two orders of magnitude. Original entry follows. — The phone normalizer's "international form with the `+` left off" branch misfires wherever a **national** number legitimately begins with its own calling code, which makes one country's mobile ranges resolve one digit short — inside the length bounds, so nothing rejects it. The trunk-prefixed spelling resolves correctly, so only the bare form breaks. Marked open because the docblock acknowledges the ambiguity in general and how commonly that spelling is actually typed there is unverified; a real order, or the upstream metadata, would settle it. **Closed 2026-08-11 as a deliberate limitation rather than a defect.** Telling the two spellings apart needs per-country possible lengths - a third column on the generated table - and the design decision behind #145's fix is precisely that the third column is where adopting the library beats growing this one. Recorded where it can be met rather than left implicit: named in `normalize_phone_number()`'s docblock beside the other two unmodelled classes, and in upstream row U110's "deliberately NOT modelled" clause. The trunk-prefixed spelling resolves correctly, which is what keeps it narrow. | `src/Modules/WooCommerce/Helpers.php` |
| 152 | Low (improvement / third-party contract) | **fixed** | A third-party schema that returns an **absolute** URL where the contract asks for a path gets a silently doubled link. The contract is documented in three docblocks and pinned by a consistency test — but that test iterates **built-in modules only**, so the one audience that can get it wrong is the one the guard cannot reach. Detecting an absolute path and passing it through costs one `str_starts_with` and is already safe, since the value passes the same protocol allow-list either way. **Fixed 2026-08-11** that way. Guarded against the leniency becoming a second place the documentation domain can be written down: the built-ins are still held to the path form by the consistency test, and a path merely *containing* "http" is still resolved against the base - both asserted. 4 new assertions, **probe-verified red** against the pre-fix code. | `src/Admin/Docs.php` |

**The theme, and it is the same one four reviews running: the fix that did not reach its
sibling.** #146 is a rule written into a comment while the line three above it breaks the rule;
#147 is a ledger of ten call sites with an eleventh outside the grep that built it; #149 is a
method move whose prose references were not carried. Each of the three fixes is *correct where
it touched*. The whole yield is what each one did not reach — which is, by now, less a recurring
finding than a property of how this codebase gets changed, and an argument for making
"enumerate the family, then grep the old name" a step rather than a habit.

**The Medium is worth reading as the counter-example.** #145 is not a sibling left behind; it is
a **default that asserts more than it can carry**. A lookup table plus a fallback is a claim that
the fallback is right for everything unlisted, and the docblock even states the claim in words
("every other territory … either uses `0` or has no trunk prefix at all, which is what the
default covers") — where the two halves of that sentence are not the same thing and the second
one is the counterexample. Generalized as **RI-22**.

| 141 | Low (robustness; no site actor — needs a third-party filter) | **fixed** | The guard was private to one class, so eight sibling sinks kept the defect it was written for. `wp_json_encode()` returns `false` for a value it cannot encode and PHP renders `false` as `''`, so a sink concatenating it emits an empty literal — a `SyntaxError` that takes the whole `<script>` block rather than the one value. #85 fixed exactly this in `ContainerCode::json_literal()` and left it `private` with one caller, while the plugin's **primary** data layer sink sat nine lines away with the raw concatenation. Probe-verified triggers: `INF`/`NAN`, a resource, over-deep nesting; invalid UTF-8 is **not** one (`wp_json_encode()` repairs it), which is what puts this out of A0's reach. **A tenth site was found while fixing** — `Axeptio::add_head_js()` builds the literal in a *separate statement*, so the same-line grep that produced the ledger never selected it: PA-7's "grep the wrappers" lesson, on this review's own ledger. Fixed by promoting the guard to `ScriptTag::json_literal()` (public static, 10 callers, private copy deleted rather than forwarded) for the assignment-position sinks, and by omitting the statement outright at the three where it is optional (RI-13). 7 regression tests, **probe-verified**: 6 of 9 go red when the guard is reverted. | `src/Frontend/ScriptTag.php`, `src/Frontend/ContainerCode.php`, `src/Frontend/DataLayer.php`, `src/Modules/ConsentMode/Axeptio.php`, `src/Modules/WooCommerce/PurchaseTracking.php`, `src/Modules/WooCommerce/PageDataLayer.php`, `src/Modules/WooCommerce/WooCommerceModule.php`, `src/Admin/SettingsPage.php`, + 4 module config sinks |
