Unreproducible because the prose says "callback" while 31 of the 64 hits are REST / Store-API / `VisitorField` / `spl_autoload_register` / `array_filter` callables. Conclusion unaffected: 0 unresolved either way. Fixed by correcting the figure **and stating the counting rule beside it**, which is what the Dead JS row does and why that one reproduces across runs. | `.security/code-review-checklist.md` |
| 143 | Low (performance / a lever that does nothing) | **fixed** | The consent gate was enqueued for **every** media tracker, including the four that fetch no SDK at all (HTML5 media, Wistia, JW Player, VideoPress — each says so in its own comment). A site tracking only self-hosted video therefore paid an extra `<script src>` on every page for a 35-byte file gating nothing, and "block `gtm4wp-media-gate` to stop every SDK request" read as a working lever while doing nothing there. Fixed with an explicit `$fetches_sdk` argument; the gate, its dependency edge **and** the expectation flag now move together, because announcing an expectation with no gate enqueued would make an SDK-fetching tracker loaded some other way read "expected but never ran" as a refusal — failing closed where nothing was refused. 2 new tests + 2 updated to the new contract; the stale "every media tracker depends on it" claim corrected in both docblocks, `CHANGELOG.md` and `readme.txt`. | `src/Modules/MediaEvents/MediaEventsModule.php` |
| 144 | Low (unverified claim → **verified, claim holds**) | **fixed** (docs) | Raised at Conf. 0.4 and open: `enqueue_media_tracker()`'s docblock asserts the trackers keep `strategy => 'defer'`, but #134's fix had since attached a `wp_add_inline_script( …, 'before' )` to every tracker handle, and no WordPress source exists in this checkout to check whether that demotes them to blocking. **Settled against core** (`WP_Scripts::filter_eligible_strategies()`): only an inline script in the **'after'** position forces blocking — *"Handles with inline scripts attached in the 'after' position cannot be delayed"* — because an `'after'` inline would run before the delayed script. Every inline this module attaches uses `'before'`, so the claim is **correct** and no behaviour changes. Recorded in the docblock with the core function named, so nobody re-derives it, plus the re-check trigger if any inline ever moves to `'after'`. PA-16's own rule applied to the claim that shipped beside its fix. | `src/Modules/MediaEvents/MediaEventsModule.php` |

**The theme, and it is this system's oldest one: the fix that did not reach its siblings.**
#141 is #85 eight sinks later; the guard existed, was correct, and was `private`. What makes
it worth more than a Low is the **shape of the miss** — the sibling that *does* guard
(`DataLayer::wrapper_fragments()`) sits **three lines above** the sibling that did not, in
the same method. And the review's own ledger then repeated the mistake in miniature: it
listed 9 sites because it grepped for the encoder and the concatenation *on one line*, and
`Axeptio` builds the literal in a separate statement one file away. That is PA-7's #74
lesson — the class survives inside a shape the grep does not select — landing on the
reviewer rather than the code.

**#140 confirmed in the new session it required.** R17 fixed the three agent definitions'
frontmatter key and recorded, correctly, that it could verify only the file: agent
definitions resolve at session start, so the fixing session still reported the pre-fix
listing. R18 is that new session, and the listing reports `code-reviewer` →
*Read, Grep, Glob, Bash*, `test-reviewer` → *Read, Grep, Glob, Bash*, `upstream-reviewer` →
*Read, Grep, Glob, Bash, WebFetch, WebSearch* — exactly the `tools:` line each file
declares, where before the fix all three reported "All tools". **The restriction is
applied.** That also settles #135's open question: `tools:` *restricts*, it does not
*grant*, so the agent definitions add no pre-approved execution surface.

**#81 has zero instances in the enforced allowlist in this checkout** (not "fixed" — PA-14:
a fix to a git-ignored config never propagates to another checkout, and the inherent
baseline of `composer phpcs` / `npm run build` / `phpunit` is unchanged; those now prompt
rather than being pre-approved). `.claude/settings.json` holds **6** entries, all
`WebFetch(domain:…)`; `settings.local.json` holds 3 (`Bash(ls -d …)` pinned and read-only,
`WebSearch`, one `WebFetch`). **#77 and #118 unchanged and open.** CI unchanged and
contained. **The repository's own history remains unswept.**

**FP-1 re-derived** (oldest un-re-derived, R11). Conclusion holds; R11's *correction* of the
stated mechanism also holds and is worth repeating because the entry's wording still invites
the wrong reading — `sanitize_rules()` puts `script` in the **allowed** element list, so
`wp_kses()` does not strip a `</script>` inside a value. `JSON_HEX_TAG` **at the caller** is
the control. Its precondition re-derived across all **12** script-sink callers; holds at each.

**Ledgers re-derived, with their rules:** 12 script-sink call sites · 36 `wp_json_encode`
occurrences · 28 `esc_js` hits (13 comments, remainder all pile (a)) · 151 option constants,
0 unread · 20 bundles + 3 libs, 0 orphans · 8 `global $…` statements · 64 array-callback
names, 0 unresolved (#142) · 0 forwarding wrappers · 4 PA-7 sites, all guarded.

**Baseline:** PHP **929 tests / 3095 assertions green** (was 920/3064 at review time; +9 from this session's regression tests), `phpcs`

**Every finding is in the base commit, and both Mediums are in the same fix.** The

**The theme: a control that names three levers, of which one works.** #125's consent gate
is the reference implementation PA-16 now points at, and two of its three documented
levers do not hold. Both fail **open**, both are promised in `CHANGELOG.md` and
`readme.txt`, and neither produces an error — which is the shape RI-20 named: if this
were completely broken, the site owner would see exactly what they would see if it were
working.

**The maintainer's challenge on #133 inverted the finding, and the correction is worth more
than the finding was.** Asked whether the proposed fix was really a fix, given that the gate
exists so that *no third-party integration is required*, the answer turned out to be no —
and neither is the gate. `gtm4wp-media-gate.js` is served from the site's **own** domain, so
a consent manager's third-party-domain blocklist can never match it; pulling that lever
takes a rule naming the handle, which is the deliberate configuration the design was meant
to avoid needing. **The zero-configuration protection is somewhere else and already works.**
Every SDK-fetching tracker selects on `iframe[src*="<vendor domain>"]`, and `ensureSdk()` is
reachable only from `if ( present.length )` or the observer's `if ( matched || inner.length )`
— so a consent manager blocking the **embed** by domain (`src` → `data-src`, or a placeholder
node), which is the ordinary zero-config thing these plugins do, leaves the selector nothing
to match and the vendor is never contacted. Consent restores the `src`, the shared observer
sees the mutation, tracking resumes.

That makes #125's framing an overstatement in the other direction: runtime injection did not
cost the zero-config posture, it **improved** it, because before the change PHP enqueued the
vendor SDK regardless and a CMP blocking only the iframe still leaked the request. Both #133
and #134 concern a lever only a configured party can pull, which is why #133 was re-rated Low
and closed as documentation. **The property now doing the real work is undocumented and
untested** — see the follow-up finding under discussion — and the change that would destroy
it looks exactly like a bug fix: widening a selector to `iframe[data-src*=…]` or a
placeholder class so that consent-blocked embeds are tracked. Method note: the finding was
raised, and the *fix* was wrong, for the same reason — both reasoned about the levers the
gate names and neither asked what the consent plugin does to the **embed**. The lens that
found it was the maintainer asking what the feature was for.

**FP-2 re-derived (oldest un-re-derived FP, and its blessed code was in the base diff).**
Conclusion holds and so does the mechanism, which is not always the case — FP-1's stated
mechanism failed re-derivation in R11 while its conclusion stood. Both halves check out
here: the sink really is `wp_add_inline_script()` with no ampersand restore, and the
"hardened anyway" claim is real at both encoders. The re-derivation also produced a new
**RI-4 pile-(a)** member worth recording so the pile is not empty by default —
`DataLayer.php:269`'s `esc_js( $datalayer_name )`, a bare unquoted identifier that has
already passed the shared grammar, so the escaper is a no-op rather than a defect.

**One ledger reproduced across runs for the first time since #115/#132.** The script-sink
caller count re-derives to **12** with R16's stated counting rule, matching R16 exactly.
That is what writing the rule beside the number buys, and it is the same reason the Dead
JS row has reproduced three runs running.

### Report 16: `.security/code-review-report-2026-08-10-0813.md`

**Reviewed at:** `1b4445f` · **Base:** `707a3a1` (range `707a3a1..1b4445f`, **43 commits**,
124 files, +10 075 / −1 111 — the largest range this system has reviewed) · **Fixes
landed:** `b8448cb` (code, tests, changelog) + this close-out — **the base for the next
review is `b8448cb`**, and by the standing note at the top of this log that makes its own
production code something the next run has to read deliberately rather than inherit as
reviewed. Recorded at commit time, which makes it two reviews running.

**As reviewed: no Critical, no High, 2 Medium + 8 Low (#123–#132).** #128 closed
`wontfix` by maintainer decision (a beta1→beta2-only defect). Everything else fixed.

**The theme: every finding lives in the space between two files that each read as correct
on their own.** A predicate copied one file from the shared one #114 consolidated it into,
already drifted in the anchor its own docblock calls load-bearing (#123). A class docblock
still promising "no new per-page request is added" while the method three hundred lines
below explains the request it added (#124). An option description that stopped matching
its emitter while every sibling description was updated in the same range (#126). An agent
definition adding three pre-approved dispatchers without touching the open ledger that
counts them (#129). Reading either file alone tells you nothing is wrong.

**Two calls worth recording, because both inverted a first draft.**

- **#124 was raised from PA-11 alone and then verified against the outside world at the
  maintainer's prompt — which made it much stronger, and retracted the fix I had
  proposed.** WooCommerce's own 7.8 release disabled cart fragments on all routes for
  exactly this cost; neither the official `woocommerce-google-analytics-integration` nor
  GTM Kit touches the API at all; the ecosystem overwhelmingly *dequeues* it. The premise
  was then checked rather than inherited: WooCommerce's `cart-fragments.js` has **no
  empty-cart bail-out**, so an ungated enqueue really does cost every visitor a round
  trip. But the **strongest source turned out to be this repository** —
  `PageDataLayer::oneshot_wc()` already gates the other delivery channel and its docblock
  states the consequence in as many words, 400 lines from the code that did not follow it.
  My original predicate ("a session/cart cookie, or `WC()->cart` being non-empty") was
  wrong twice: gating on the cart drops `customerData` for a logged-in customer with an
  empty cart, and reading `WC()->cart` performs the very session load the gate exists to
  avoid. Fixed with a presence-only predicate over login state + cart cookie + session
  cookie, and only the *delivering* half is gated — the runtime always loads, so the first
  add-to-cart still delivers via WooCommerce's own add-to-cart response.
- **#125's first draft overstated the finding, and the correction changed the fix.** I
  wrote that runtime SDK injection "removes every server-side lever". It does not: the
  tracker bundle is its own script handle, so dequeuing or blocking `gtm4wp-<provider>`
  stops the vendor request by stopping the bundle. What actually disappeared is narrower —
  the **vendor-named URL** is no longer in the page, so a consent manager keyed to
  `player.vimeo.com` has nothing to match. Fixed with a `gtm4wp_media_sdk_blocked` filter
  consulted at the fetch (not at the scan, so a veto costs the vendor request and nothing
  else), plus documenting the per-provider handle lever. The maintainer's "ghost script"
  proposal was assessed and **not** implemented: it is the only option that restores
  zero-configuration CMP blocking, but it costs a real HTTP request per page and is
  fail-closed, so it wants the live CMP measurement first — recorded as the open decision
  below.

Recorded rather than waved through because RI-18's litmus says to read the odd sanitizer. | `src/Admin/Notices.php` |
| 132 | Low | fixed | Ledger accuracy, in the patterns file this time: **FP-1's caller ledger was wrong three ways at once** — 16 where the tree has 12 (it counted grep hits, not calls), naming `Axeptio` and `ProductData`, which only *mention* the sink in comments, while omitting the real caller `PageDataLayer`. FP-1's conclusion holds and was re-derived; the ledger is the one thing R11 told the next reviewer to re-derive instead of re-reading. **It survived because #115 fixed this same figure in the Coverage Matrix's sweep row and left the copy in the patterns file untouched** — one measurement in two of our own files, one corrected and one not. The counting rule now sits beside the number. | `.security/code-review-patterns.md` |

**The maintainer chose option A, and it shipped.** `js/frontend/gtm4wp-media-gate.js` is a
real enqueued script that every media tracker declares as a dependency and whose only
statement raises a flag; blocking or dequeuing that one handle withholds every media SDK
request while the trackers keep running for the players already on the page. All three
implementation facts the assessment named are in the code: it has a **real `src`** (a
src-less handle never reaches `script_loader_tag`, the mechanism it exists to expose), it
is a **dependency** rather than merely enqueued alongside (or the gate is a load-order
race), and it is **fail-closed**, so it ships a diagnostic - a `console.warn` under the
existing `gtm4wp_console_log` option, because an optimiser dropping one tiny "unused" file
would otherwise end all media tracking with no error and no red test (#121's lesson).

**A fourth fact turned up in the building, and it is the one worth keeping.** A bare
`! allowed` check cannot tell *"the gate was blocked"* from *"no gate is in play at all"* -
a tracker loaded standalone, or a unit test. Reading the second as the first fails closed
on a case that is not a refusal, so PHP prints a companion `gtm4wp_media_gate_expected`
flag in a **separate inline tag on the gate's own handle**: a consent manager rewrites the
`src` tag and leaves the inline one standing, which is exactly the state that has to read
as "refused". Put that flag inside the gate file instead and blocking it erases the
evidence it was ever expected - the feature would fail **open**, in the one case it exists
for.

**Probe note, because it caught a defect in the tests rather than in the code.** Neutering
the gate left two of the new cases green: `ensureSdk()` also returns early when `isReady()`
is true, so an `isReady: () => true` fixture makes a withholding assertion pass against a
build with no gate logic in it at all. Both were rewritten to `() => false` and split from
the "still wires the page" property, which is the assertion that genuinely wants `true`.
**A guard has to be probed, not merely exercised** - and the probe tests the test as much
as the code.

### Report 15: `.security/code-review-report-2026-08-05-1325.md`

**Reviewed at:** `80e116b` · **Base:** `4ce7537` (range `4ce7537..80e116b`, 3 commits —
R14's own fix commit plus two test-suite commits) · **Fixes landed:** `707a3a1` —
**the base for the next review is `707a3a1`**, and by the standing note at the top of this
log that makes its own production code something the next run has to read deliberately
rather than inherit as reviewed. Recorded at the same time as the commit, which breaks a
five-review run of leaving this line unfilled.

*(Filename note: this report's timestamp sorts before Report 14's, whose filename did not
match its creation time either. Reports are ordered by number and by `Reviewed at:`, which
is what the next run reads.)*

R14 left `Fixes:` unfilled for the fifth review running, so by the standing rule above the
base stayed R14's recorded `Reviewed at:` and **R14's entire fix session fell inside this
range** — which is again where most of the yield came from. The production diff is three
files, all of them R14's #108/#110/#112 fixes; **all three are correct where they touch**,
and every premise behind them was re-derived rather than read (the block-checkout claim
behind #108's `wp_script_is( 'enqueued' )` guard was traced to the two bundles and the
enqueue/`wp_head` priority ordering, including the `LOADEARLY` priority-2 case).
**As reviewed: no Critical/High, 1 Medium + 7 Low (#114–#121). Final, after the fix
session: 1 High + 2 Medium + 6 Low (#114–#122).**

**The review's own worst call is in its own tables, and it is worth more than the rest of
the run.** #121 was filed **Low, at 0.5 confidence, as an RI-13 convention cleanup** — an
empty companion key that "is probably inert". The reasoning was *the feature ships and
nobody has complained, so the empty value must be harmless*. During the fix session the
maintainer asked whether anything could confirm that at either end and supplied a live
container URL; replaying the vendor runtime's own functions against the plugin's output
inverted it in minutes. An empty array is **truthy in JavaScript**, so the empty companion
was an allowlist that *had been set*, and the blocking mode had been disabling the **whole
container** — in released 1.x and in 2.0 — rather than the selected entities. Re-rated
**High**. The same question produced **#122**. So the lesson is not a lens this system was
missing; it is that **a low-confidence finding is a request to go and measure, not a
finding to file small** — and that the cheapest place to measure a one-ended contract is
the other party's shipped runtime, which is downloadable.

**The theme: "re-derived" is a claim like any other.** R14's whole report argued that a
ledger is a measurement rather than a fact, and fixed one figure (#111) on that basis. Two
more figures in the *same sweep row and the same session* did not survive re-derivation,
and a third was invalidated by its own commit — so the class now has three distinct shapes:
**wrong when written** (#115), **correct-then-self-invalidated** (#116), and **a category
the rule says must not exist** (#117 — three `esc_js` string values filed under
"constrained by validation", which #110 had ruled out as a third pile one day earlier).
The one thing that *did* reproduce across two runs is the Dead-JS row, and it reproduced
because R14 wrote its counting rule down beside the count.

| # | Sev | Status | Summary | File(s) |
|---|-----|--------|---------|---------|
| 114 | Medium | fixed | Correctness/validation (**not** a security finding — A4→A4): the data-layer variable-name option's allow-list accepts a character that is not legal in a JavaScript identifier, while the value is emitted as a **bare identifier** at 10+ script sinks. A name the settings screen affirmatively accepts therefore makes every GTM4WP script block a `SyntaxError` — container still loads, all tracking silently gone, no notice. Probe-verified. The same allow-list ships on `origin/1.x`, so this is not a 2.0 regression. Fix is to reuse the identifier predicate the container code already applies to third-party global-var names, rather than keep a second, wider rule (PA-2's share-the-predicate). | `src/Modules/Container/AdminSchema.php`, `src/Frontend/DataLayer.php` |
| 115 | Low | fixed | Ledger accuracy (#111's class, one review later, same sweep row): two figures asserted as re-derived are wrong on the tree — the `print_script_block()`/`print_markup_block()` caller count (recorded 17, actual 12, and wrong since at least R12 when measured across shas) and the RI-13 row's `global $…` count (recorded 8, actual 7). **No escaping defect**: all 12 callers were read and every data-bearing one hex-encodes upstream. The cost is that the caller count is the denominator FP-1's precondition is verified against, so a figure nobody can reproduce makes that verification unfalsifiable. | `.security/code-review-checklist.md` |
| 116 | Low | fixed | Ledger self-invalidation — distinct from #115 in that the figure was **correct when derived**: the fix commit rewrote the Duplication row's `wp_json_encode` call-site count *and*, in the same commit, added the next call site (its own #110 encoder swap). Every arithmetic step was right; the tree moved underneath it. Rule extracted: re-derive a ledger **last**, after the session's code is staged. | `.security/code-review-checklist.md`, `src/Admin/Notices.php` |
| 117 | Low | fixed (partial by design - see the fix note) | Ledger under-selection (RI-4): the two-pile `esc_js` ledger — rewritten by #110 the previous day with the explicit rule that "the value happens to be harmless" is **not** a third category — records no pile-(b) members while three sit in one file. All inert (every value is pattern-validated at or before the sink), so this is the #72/#105/#110 consistency class, not XSS. | 118 | Low (D0→D1, no added reach) | open (recorded; maintainer decision; **three** hooks since 2026-08-10, not two — #129) | Toolchain (PA-14): two SessionStart hooks read **worktree-resolved** instruction files into the agent's context on every session start, in a session holding #81's pre-approved dispatchers. The sweep's bullet 2 asks whether a hook *executes* a worktree path; these only *read* one, so the litmus structurally cannot select them — and PA-14's entry-point × sink method had only ever enumerated commands and skills, never a hook. Rated "no added reach" because the agent already resolves `CLAUDE.md`/commands/skills from the worktree; the delta is that two non-standard files are promoted into the same trust position. Only resolving them from outside the worktree is enforcement; a prose preamble is mitigation, which PA-14 already says. | `.claude/settings.json` |
| 119 | Low | fixed | FP packaging + verdict mechanism, re-derived under the oldest-first rule (R14 nominated **FP-4**). Two halves. **(a) FP-4 had no entry at all** — a Quick Index row was the entire waiver, so the preamble's requirement that every FP state verifiable properties and name what invalidates it had nowhere to be met. That is the mirror of #113: there the body was current and the index row stale; here the index row was all there was. **An FP with no entry can only be re-read, never re-derived.** **(b) The conclusion holds** — still do not flag the AMP delegated sink — but the stated mechanism is not what makes it safe, the same outcome as R11's FP-1 re-derivation. The real guard is a serializer default in the delegated-to plugin, one flag away from being removed by a dependency this repo does not control, and neither the FP nor the module docblock would prompt anyone to look if it were. Fixed by writing the entry with the corrected mechanism and its re-check trigger. **Next review re-derives FP-5** (oldest, and its condition 3 carries open #78). | `.security/code-review-patterns.md`, `src/Modules/Amp/AmpModule.php` |
| 120 | Low (security, A4 — hardening, no live break-out) | fixed | Robustness, RI-17 read backwards: #112's fix null-gates the parsed URL parts **before** the escaper, but the escaper itself can return an empty string for a scheme outside a filterable allow-list — re-creating the half-built value the gate was added to prevent. Unverified (needs a site with a narrowed protocol filter), stated as a shape. Generalizable half: **a null-gate placed upstream of an escaper does not cover the escaper's own failure mode.** One-line fix. | `src/Modules/MediaEvents/MediaEventsModule.php` |
| 122 | Medium | fixed | Integration contract drift, found by checking #121 against the vendor's documentation instead of reasoning about it: the module wrote its data-layer keys under names that appear **nowhere** in Google's current documentation for the feature, nor in the older page it superseded — with no statement anywhere that the legacy names are supported, deprecated, or otherwise. The module's own docblocks cited that page as their authority in three places while emitting names it does not contain. Not a live defect (the runtime has read both pairs), and that is exactly what makes it worth rating: **the plugin writes the key and something else entirely decides whether to read it, so a wrong name fails silently** — every tag runs unrestricted while the settings screen still shows the restriction in place, and no test on this side can see it. Switched to the documented names. Ships a test that pins them and rejects the legacy pair outright, so "also emit the old ones for compatibility" cannot creep back in unnoticed. **Residual, stated honestly: this moves the feature onto documented ground; it does not verify the feature works.** That still wants one live check against a real container. | `src/Modules/Blacklist/BlacklistModule.php` |
| 121 | ~~Low~~ **High** (re-rated 2026-08-05 after measurement; correctness, **not** a security finding - it fails closed) | fixed | Silent total feature failure, found by replaying the vendor runtime instead of reasoning about it. Publishing an empty companion restriction key alongside the populated one did not mean "no restriction of that kind": an empty array is truthy in JavaScript, the runtime treats it as a list that HAS been set, and its allow-test then rejects every entity. Enabling the feature in its blocking mode therefore disabled the whole container rather than the selected entities - in released 1.x and in 2.0 before this fix. Fails **closed**, which is why it survived: it reads as an over-eager restriction, not a defect, and the field description already warns the feature can affect tag deployment. The other mode was unaffected. Fixed by emitting only the selected mode's key; ships a test that asserts ABSENCE (not emptiness) of the companion under every name the runtime consults. **Originally filed Low as an RI-13 convention cleanup at 0.5 confidence, on the reasoning that the feature evidently worked so the empty key must be inert - the inference was backwards, and measuring it took ten minutes.** | `src/Modules/Blacklist/BlacklistModule.php` |

**Fix session (2026-08-05), landed as `707a3a1`.** Eight of the nine fixed; #118 left
recorded by maintainer decision (it is rated "no added reach" over a baseline the agent
already has). The session produced **two findings the review itself did not have** —
#122, and #121's re-rating from Low to High — both from one maintainer question about a
0.5-confidence item. Calls worth recording:

- **#121/#122 came from reading the vendor's shipped runtime, and that is now a repeatable
  move.** Documentation settled the key *names* and ran out at the *values*: neither Google
  page says what an empty restriction list means. `curl` of a live container, `grep` for the
  literal, and the consuming functions lifted verbatim into a throwaway probe answered it
  outright. Recorded in **RI-20** with the two halves that generalize: a falsy placeholder
  is not neutral across a language boundary (PHP's empty `array()` is JavaScript's truthy
  `[]`), and a fails-closed security feature that is completely broken looks exactly like
  one that is working, so no bug report is ever coming.

- **#114's fix is one shared predicate, not a corrected regex.** The codebase already held
  the right rule — `ContainerCode::header_top()` validates third-party global-variable
  names against the JavaScript identifier grammar, with a comment saying `esc_js()` cannot
  validate an identifier — ten files from the option that had the wrong one. Both now call
  `ContainerRows::is_valid_js_identifier()`, and the resolution (including the fallback)
  lives in `ContainerRows::datalayer_name()` so the save side, the frontend reader, the 1.x
  `$GLOBALS` mirror and the new admin notice cannot drift (PA-2 + RI-14). The **read side
  is where the fix actually bites**: the sanitizer only runs on save, and 1.x accepted
  hyphenated names that the migration stores verbatim, so an upgrading site arrives with a
  value only the sink can reject. Ships the notice too — a silent substitution would have
  traded one undiagnosable failure for another (#60's rule: validation without a signal is
  half a fix).
- **#117 is fixed where it is free and *recorded* where it is not, which is the rule's own
  remedy.** `header_top()`'s name literal moved to `json_literal()` (byte-identical). The
  four `esc_js` calls in the container-loader snippet did **not**: `json_literal()` emits
  double quotes and that block is Google's snippet reproduced byte for byte in single
  quotes, under byte-exact tests (BE-1). They are now documented in place — with each of
  the four allow-lists that makes `esc_js` a provable no-op named, and the condition that
  would force the migration stated — rather than left to be re-decided. #110's rule says a
  constrained value belongs in pile (b) *with a note*; this is that, not an exemption.
- **#121 changes 1.x-parity data-layer output, so it is a `* Changed:` bullet, not a
  silent cleanup.** 1.x emits both keys (`origin/1.x:public/frontend.php:588-589`). Three
  existing tests asserted the empty sibling key — they pinned the old contract correctly,
  so they were rewritten to assert the new one **in both directions** (`assertArrayNotHasKey`),
  not deleted. R14's rule applied: when a fix breaks a test, read the test before trusting it.
- **Every guard was probe-verified to discriminate, not just to pass.** Reverting
  `JS_IDENTIFIER_PATTERN` to the exact 1.x regex turns 8 of the new cases red; removing the
  `'' === $origin` guard produces `origin=` with nothing after it in the failure output;
  restoring both blacklist keys fails all three rewritten cases. A first probe attempt
  produced a *malformed* regex rather than the 1.x one and was re-run — a broken constant
  failing the tests proves only that they read the constant.

**Verification highlights (no findings):** all eight whole-repo sweeps re-run and
re-derived from scratch, each recorded **with its counting rule** so the next run produces
a comparable figure. Mechanical: **57** PHP files carrying the ABSPATH guard out of 59
tracked non-test files (the two exempt are the main plugin file and `uninstall.php`, which
correctly guards on `WP_UNINSTALL_PLUGIN`), **149** constants with zero dead, **63** hook
callbacks all resolving, **19** JS bundles none dead with libs at 1/2/12 importers,
**31** superglobal reads all unslashed and validated, **24** `wp_json_encode` call sites
with every script-context one hex-flagged, **12** script-sink callers with every
data-bearing one hex-encoding upstream, **0** `$wpdb`/`unserialize()`/`eval()`/entity-decode,
**0** order `get_post_meta()`, **0** forwarding wrappers, **4** PA-7 data-capable
`preg_replace` sites all guarded. Surface grep reconciles exactly; `jest.config.js` needed
no new matrix row because #101 had already widened the Git-hooks-&-CI label to cover any
tool config a hook or CI step feeds to an executable. **Sibling asymmetry checked and
dismissed with a reason**: the three `Plugin::instance()->frontend()` call sites disagree
on null-checking, and that is not the RI-19 shape — `boot_frontend()` assigns the property
before calling `->boot()`, so it is non-null by construction at all three. Deep-pass
re-audit of the oldest `[x]` rows R14's pass did not reach (Blacklist, ClientDeviceData,
AMP, UserEvents, Plugin Bootstrap) produced #119 and #121 and nothing else. Also fixed in
passing: two sweep rows carried a stray unescaped `|` from R14's nesting, which split them
into a phantom fourth column. Baseline: **PHP 767 tests / 2488 assertions green** (was
706/2325), **JS 404 tests / 30 suites green** (was 302/23 — the eight new admin component
suites), `phpcs` **exit 0** repo-wide with warnings blocking. **No `js/` source changed**,
so RI-9 does not apply and no `build/` regeneration was required.

### Report 3: fix session note

**Fix session (2026-07-13):** all six findings #19-24 **fixed** (#19 was resolved in the working tree by dropping the unused `player` assignment; #20-24 fixed in this session). #20 was upgraded Low→correctness once analysis showed `duration===0, time>0` fires `Infinity` milestones (not a benign `NaN` no-op). #24 refactor migrated all 11 trackers to the two new shared helpers with byte-preserved push payloads. New regression tests: youtube (enablejsapi query + duration-0 + the pre-existing undeclared-`player` guard), soundcloud (duration-0), wistia + spotify (double-init), and 7 `native-video-params` lib-helper tests; 2 `MediaEventsModuleTest` cases for #23. Verified: **JS 150 tests / 16 suites green** (was 138), **PHP 253 tests green**, `npm run build` + `npm run lint:js` clean, `phpcs` clean. CHANGELOG updated. Findings #23/#21 also fixed a pre-existing (Phase-0/Phase-2) functional gap, not just the new code.
